### 4.2 The order of the checks at every door

**Step 0 (D2729 Q9 — §21.1's block): N14 is answered. The saver gives up on a send that has had no answer for a set time and shows "Not saved" with Retry (the new step K4a); the waiting door of step 3 is built at every door; the other answer is not built.**

1. **Is the press a change at all?** The squadron already in view picked (`u === UNIT`); a person who may not switch
   (`mayViewAsMember()` false); a line that enters nothing and opens neither a page nor a list — `enterForLine`
   answers `'here'` or `'no'` for it, and it is an input since deleted ("That input is gone"), a posting of a man with
   no row left, or his own account's line. → exactly as today, nothing asked, nothing said (pinned, P16). **The same
   line of a squadron he can enter is a change:** `openNotice` calls `enterForLine` before it looks at what the line is
   about, so today it enters that squadron, and after Build 1 it is refused like any line.
2. **The guard.** `refuse(door, to, opts)` — one reason at a time, in `GUARD_ORDER`: the window, then the open row,
   then Retry (D2627 A10). Refused → the note, and the press ends having changed nothing: no flag, no page, no line
   marked read, no list closed by it, no card spent, **no show-only window closed — and no window, question or toast
   opened after it** (§4.5).
3. **A save still going** (D2627 A9) — at every door, the page door too: where `saveSettling()` and not
   `saveRefused()`, the door waits for the saver's own answer and starts again from step 1 — landed: it goes on;
   "Not saved": step 2 refuses with the Retry note. Each door either owns what follows it (`then`) or is handed the tap
   to run again (`again`), so each can wait (§4.5). **Never a wait without end, and never a timer of the door's own:**
   the wait ends at the saver's own answer (`storage/savewait.ts` — its state leaves "settling" when the save lands or
   it gives up and shows "Not saved"); B20 proves it with a save that never answers, and if the saver itself has no
   end the builder reports that before this step is built (D87: wait on what is needed, never a fixed time).
   **(reading), named for him:** a press that is waiting is dropped if he presses anything else meanwhile — otherwise
   a pick could land seconds later, under his next press.
   **A prerequisite, not yet met (Astra 7 of the third read; read on this branch):** `storage/postman.ts` answers
   `'saving'` for as long as a send is in flight and nothing ends a send that never answers — so today "the wait ends
   at the saver's own answer" is untrue for a connection that hangs, and the door would wait without end, against
   D2627 A9. **N14 — a switch point, put to him because he would see it:** (default, the reviser's recommendation,
   on no page yet) the saver itself gives up on a send that has had no answer for a set time and shows "Not saved"
   with Retry, as a refused save does — a change to how saving behaves everywhere, with its own test and its own
   read (saved data: D1411's reader beside the code read); the other answer: no wait is built — a press made while
   "Saving…" shows is refused with a note of its own, against A9's letter. **The waiting door (this step, at every
   door) is not built until one is chosen**; steps 3 to 5 are otherwise complete without it, each door then treating
   a save in flight as today.
4. **The Tracker's question**, where the press would take the Tracker off unsaved chart edits — at a pick as today,
   and at the view switch always, both ways (D2627 A20).
5. **After the question is answered Save or Discard, step 2 again** — a window or a failed save may have arrived while
   it stood (§11, risk 1).
6. **`closeShown()`** — the windows that only show things close (D2596, D2597) — **then the change, the two in one
   synchronous run inside the final entry, at every door: `enterPicked`, the view switch's own body, `enterForLine`'s
   entering run, and `landOnOwnPage` after its second ask — never earlier** (Astra 5, Fable 10: a Stay, or a refusal
   after the question, cannot bring back a window already closed). Logic's and
   Quals' edit mode end in the same step (D2627 A4 — §18.1). **(reading):** D2596 and D2597 name a pick and the view
   switch; a bell line, a bell tap and a card that change squadron close them the same way, on D2403's "a bell line
   that would take him to another squadron follows the same rule".

### 4.3 `state/pick.ts`

- `pickSquadron(u, opts?)`: after `if (u === UNIT) return`, `if (refuse('pick', u, opts)) return`; then the wait of
  §4.2 step 3. In the `ask.then`, before `enterPicked(u)`: `if (u === UNIT || refuse('pick', u, opts)) return`.
  `enterPicked` gains two lines ahead of `enterSquadron`: `closeShown()` and the edit modes' end (§18.1).
- `pickWillAsk(u)`: false where the guard would refuse (the ☰ menu reads it to decide whether to close "for the
  question").
- New `pickRefused(u): boolean` = `u !== UNIT && !!refusalAt('pick', u)` — for the ☰ menu, which must know before it
  touches its own list (as `pickWillAsk` exists for the same reason).
- `ui/Drawer.tsx DrawerSquadron pick`: `setOpen(null)`; then where `pickRefused(u)` or `pickWillAsk(u)` — `setDrawer(false);
  notify()`; else the keys back on the button; then `void pickSquadron(u, { from: 'drawer' })`. So a refused pick closes
  the menu and the note stands at the foot with the window in sight, as drawn (`p3-p`; D2627 A16). A pick that goes
  through leaves the menu open, as today (D2384).
- `ui/SquadronMenu.tsx pick`: unchanged but for `{ from: 'bar' }` — it already closes its list and puts the keys back on
  `#sqnMenuBtn`, which is where the note then hangs.

### 4.4 `state/store.ts switchRoleView(opts?)`

Today it is one synchronous body that asks nothing. It becomes:

1. `if (!mayViewAsMember()) return`; `const toMember = isAdmin()`.
2. `if (refuse(toMember ? 'toMember' : 'toAdmin', null, opts)) return` — `opts.skip` for row 5, `opts.from` for the
   note's place.
3. **The Tracker's question — asked always, both ways, wherever there are unsaved chart edits (D2627 A20), on his own
   squadron too.** New in `tracker/role.js`: `trackerSwitchAsk(to, back = false)`; in `core.js` the registered
   `ask(to, back)` builds its last line as `'Save them before switching ' + (back ? 'back to ' : 'to ') + to + '?'` —
   so a pick's question is byte for byte today's. Called with `('the member view', false)` and `('the admin view',
   true)`: "You have unsaved chart edits on “2026”. Save them before switching to the member view?" `[ruled]` (D2627
   A20: "as drawn 24 Nov", `p5`) — and "…Save them before switching back to the admin view?" `[WNS]` (words-read N7:
   read by a fresh Fable, not shown to him on the page). The one predicate: `viewSwitchAsks()` = `trackerSwitchNeeds()`
   — unsaved edits, nothing about which squadron. The answers are the pick's: Stay — nothing; Discard them — the chart
   brought back to its last save (D2318, D2395), then the switch; Save them — saved, then the switch; a Save that does
   not take — he stays (`core.js saveForQuestion`). Its cost, told to him on the page: a Discard here throws away
   edits the switch itself would not have touched.
4. After Save or Discard: step 2 again, then `closeShown()`, then today's body — `switchRoleInForce`, `bumpNav`, the
   landing off Edit Schedule and Admin, `armDrop`, `setLgEdit(false)`, `clearAdminOpen`, `lwSetRole`.
5. **Where the switch moves his own pages** (`pageUnit('own')` before it is not `pageUnit('own')` after — a Plan holder
   or master admin with another squadron in view): `enterSquadron(UNIT, false, true)` **before** `notify()` — the entry
   his own press makes, so `rebindStorageUnit(own, mayHold = false)` moves the Tracker (`core.js follow` → `'moved'`) and
   a chart editor open with nothing unsaved closes as it does at a pick. Without this line the move is left to
   `maybeReenter`, whose entry is not "by pick", and `trkMayHold()` holds the Tracker under the member-view cover for
   an open editor — the state D2403 says his own switch must not leave. **The editor closing here is a change he would
   see that no ruling names**: today, on his own squadron, Edit chart layout stays open through the
   switch (pressed, P1 4), and for a Plan holder on 149 it waits under the cover (from the code). **E3 (N10) — the
   page's recommendation is this line (default); built only on his answer.** *For the builder, unproved:* the `notify()` after it runs `maybeReenter` once more with `mayHold` true
   while the Tracker's move is still under way; `follow`'s first call has already run `leaveSquadron` up to its first
   wait, which ends the editor (`endSession`), so the second call should find nothing to hold. Fable's reader could not
   fault this from reading; B14 pins the result, not the reasoning.
6. `notify()`.

`switchRoleView` now answers a promise where it asked. Its three callers ignore the result (`onClick`), as `pick`'s do.
**`ui/Drawer.tsx #drawerRole`** becomes `setDrawer(false); notify(); void switchRoleView({ from: 'drawer' })` — the menu
is closed and redrawn whether the switch goes through or is refused (row 4); the badge passes `{ from: 'bar' }`.

### 4.5 The bell and the page door

**The cut chosen, and why.** The squadron comes back through `enterForLine`'s `'no'` and through the landing (§15,
finding 1). **The plan takes one check at the page door, and makes that door run whatever the press would open after
the page.** The reasons, from the code:

- **What moves him is the landing, and the landing has one cause.** `store.ts landOnOfferedPage` runs on every
  `notify()`; where the page just opened is one the squadron in view does not offer (`!pageOffered(CURPAGE)`), it calls
  `landOnOwnPage`, which enters his home with `byPick` true. So "this press brings his own squadron back" is exactly
  "it changes the page to one `pageOffered` denies" — the door asks the predicate the landing itself acts on.
- **It has no list of callers to keep.** Seventeen non-test places outside `store.ts` call `setPage` today, in twelve
  files (counted again at the third read — Opus: `leavewar/sync.ts` four, `ui/topstate.ts` three with `main`'s SANS
  reminder, one each in ten more), six of which reach `view.setPage` directly. A door in the one function cannot be walked round, and R5 (b)
  fails the day a file reaches past it.
- **It must stand before `view.setPage`, not at the landing.** `view.setPage` itself closes things on a page change —
  ALL AVAIL, Changes, the Traffic window, the OIL question waiting on a change, the armed confirms. A check after it
  would look at a screen the press had already changed.
- **A refusal at the door is not enough by itself** (§16, finding 1). Today `ui/topstate.ts openInputOnPage` is
  `setPage('inputs'); notify(); landingAsk() … else open(false)`, and `open` runs `first()` and `setInpEdit(row)`;
  `leavewar/sync.ts openInputWindow` is `setPage('inputs')`, then `setInpEdit(row)`. Behind a door that only answered
  "no", both would open the window all the same — and with the page still 149's View-only Sched it notes itself as
  opened on 149 (`InputWindow.tsx`: off the Inputs page `pageSqn = UNIT`) and stands **live, beside the "Blocked:"
  note**, its Save writing 142's record under 149's number. The same after the landing's own question, if its promise
  answered true when the guard had just refused. **So nothing may follow a refusal:**

**`state/store.ts setPage(p, opts?, then?): PageGo` — the page door** (new; today the name is a re-export of
`view.setPage`). `PageGo` is `'went' | 'asked' | 'refused'`; `opts` is `{ door?, from?, skip?, line? }`; `then(waited)`
is what the press opens once its page is there.

1. `p === view.CURPAGE`, or `pageOffered(p)`: `view.setPage(p)`; where a `then` was handed over, `notify()` and
   `then(false)`; `'went'`. Every tab, every page of his own squadron and every sign-in goes this way, unasked.
2. Otherwise the press would bring his own squadron back. `if (refuse(opts?.door ?? 'page', home, opts)) return
   'refused'` — nothing has changed and `then` is dropped. Passed: the door and its `opts` are kept in one module
   variable; `view.setPage(p)`; `notify()` — the landing runs inside this call (today it ran on the caller's own
   `notify()`). No question pending (`LANDASK` null): `then?.(false)`; `'went'`. One pending — the Tracker's, over
   unsaved edits in the member view: `LANDASK.then(go => { if (go) then?.(true) })`; `'asked'`.
3. **`landOnOwnPage`, after its question is answered Save or Discard, asks the guard again with the door and `opts`
   it kept** (§4.2 step 5) — `if (refuse(kept.door, home, kept.opts)) return false`, before `enterSquadron`. Its
   promise answers false: nothing is entered, the page stays, `then` never runs, the note stands at the kept door's
   control.
4. A save still going (§4.2 step 3): in step 2, before the ask — `'asked'`, and the door runs itself again with the
   same `then` once the saver has answered.
5. **`closeShown()` runs inside `landOnOwnPage`, after its question and its second ask, in the run that enters his own
   squadron — not at the door** (§4.2 step 6). The earlier draft put it before `view.setPage(p)`: in B27's state the
   Tracker's question then stood over windows already closed, and a Stay could not give them back (Astra 5, Fable
   10). On step 1's road — a page the squadron in view offers — nothing changes squadron and nothing is closed but
   what `view.setPage` itself closes today.

**`state/store.ts ownSquadronFirst(opts, then): PageGo` — the same door for a press that opens a window and no page**
(the holiday's bell line: D2627 A19). Where the squadron in view is his pages' own it runs `then(false)` and answers
`'went'`. Where it is a squadron he only views, it is step 2 to 5 above without the page: the guard, the wait, the
entry `landOnOwnPage` makes (the Tracker's question with it, then the guard again), and `then` only once his own
squadron is in view. One body with the page door — the page door is `ownSquadronFirst` plus `view.setPage` — so the
two can never differ.

So `then` runs in exactly one state: **the page asked for is the page shown.** `waited` is true after a question; the
caller's own "is it still his to open" test (today's `still`) is asked there, as today.

**Two rules for a caller, one for each side of the page change:**

- **What it sets before the page — a line read, a list closed, an intent the page reads as it opens, a card spent — it
  asks about first:** `ownPageRefused(p, door, opts?, again?): boolean` — the door's own test and its note, changing
  nothing; the function's first statement, returning on true (`again` is H8's: the tap run again after the save).
- **What it does after the page — a window, a question, the toast that goes with it — it hands to the door as
  `then`.** One site tests the door's answer instead, and goes on only on `'went'`.

Every call site of the door, with its kind — the census R5 (c) pins (sixteen once the OIL list's card goes through
`openInputOnPage`; the builder counts again after `main` is in), and the two functions above them that set a mark:

| Call site | Sets before the page | Does after it | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ui/Shell.tsx nav`, `ui/Drawer.tsx go` — a tab, a ☰ page | — | a redraw; the ☰ menu closed | `last` — only offered pages are drawn (row 14) |
| `ui/Login.tsx`; `ui/logout.ts`; `probe-bridge.ts`; `topstate.ts bellTap`'s bug alert (`'help'`) | — | a redraw | `last` — his home just entered; guarded by `pageOffered`; the developer's; Help is always offered |
| `ui/interactions.ts` "make a period" | a period written, a toast | a redraw | `last` — it needs `canEditSched()`, false on a squadron he only views, so its page is always offered |
| `leavewar/sync.ts openInputsPage` | — | a redraw | `last` |
| `ui/topstate.ts openInputOnPage(iid, first, still, opts)`, exported — a bell line of an input, the bell's own OIL tap, the OIL list's card | — | the Input window, with `first` (the OIL question and its toast) | **`then`** — its whole body is `setPage('inputs', opts, waited => …today's open…)` |
| `leavewar/sync.ts openInputWindow` — the clash note | — | the Input window | **`then`** — the input found afresh inside it; it answers true ("the tap was taken") whatever the door says |
| `ui/topstate.ts openNotice` (calls the openers below) | the line marked read, the bell's list closed | — | **asks first** — door `'line'`, `{ line: n.id }` |
| `state/belltap.ts openBellRequest` (calls `openAdminUsers`) | — | the toast "1 waiting for access to 149" | **asks first** (`'bell'`), before `open()`, so the toast cannot follow a refusal inside it |
| `ui/adminopen.ts openAdminUsers` | the Users intent (`requestAdminUsers`) | a redraw | **asks first** |
| `leavewar/sync.ts openWarAt` | the war selected, Approvals and Balances put away, the ring asked for (`askRing`) | a redraw | **asks first**, where the page is not already the Leave War |
| `leavewar/sync.ts openNewPeriod` | the new-period ask (`askNewWar`) | a redraw | **asks first** — pressed only inside the Calendar window, which closes itself when its squadron is no longer the page's, so no road reaches its refusal |
| `ui/WelcomeBack.tsx` "Check my quals" | `markBackSeen()` — the card spent; the Quals row to show | a redraw | `last` — the card is drawn only on his own squadron (D2628 C4, §18.5), where Quals is always offered; the door is its belt, and R5 (c) pins that the card's own gate stands before it |
| `state/undo-wire.ts snapView` | (it runs inside the step) | `land.then()` | **`answer`** — only on `'went'`. No road reaches it (row 13); if one is found, `globalUndo` asks before the step |

The ask sits in the function that sets the mark, not only in its caller — Quals' "+ Add person" and the bell both
reach `openAdminUsers`; where a caller has asked already the second ask passes in the same turn. Inside `store.ts`
the landings, the entry and the session reset keep calling `view.setPage`: the app's own moves, never a press (row
20). `leavewar/sync.ts`, `ui/WelcomeBack.tsx`, `ui/Login.tsx`, `ui/interactions.ts`, `state/undo-wire.ts` and
`probe-bridge.ts` import the page door in place of `view.setPage` (R5 (b)). **A forgotten ask-first line leaves** a
line read or a card spent beside a note, caught by its own test (B28) — never a window. **A forgotten `then` is
stopped by** R5 (c), by the checker's second check under every test (§3.4), and by B26 and B27.

**`state/belltap.ts enterForLine(u, again, door = 'line', opts?)`**
- `'here'` and `'no'` answer as today, with nothing asked: what such a tap then opens is the page door's to judge.
- Where it would enter (`ownPageMayBe(u)`): `if (refuse(door, u, opts)) return 'refused'`, before the Tracker's
  question. **`closeShown()` is called in the run that enters, immediately before `enterSquadron`** — after the
  question, since `again` re-runs the whole tap (§4.2 step 6). `opts` carries the pressed line, so the note hangs under it, and a door's `skip`. `again` re-runs the
  whole tap after Save or Discard, so step 5 of §4.2 needs no code here; the same `again` serves H8's wait.
- `LineEntry` gains `'refused'`.

**`ui/topstate.ts openNotice`**
- `const o = { line: n.id }`; `enterForLine(n.u, again, 'line', o)`; `if (where === 'asked' || where === 'refused')
  return` — it stands before `markNoticeRead` and `setBellList(false)`.
- Then one new function, `lineOpens(n, where): string | null` — the page this tap will open, read from the branches
  already there: none for his own account's line, for a posting or Leave War line where `where === 'no'`, for a
  holiday's line (it opens the OIL list, no page — its own branch, next), for a posting whose man has no row, for an
  input since deleted; `'admin'` for a posting, `'leavewar'` for a Leave War line, `'inputs'` for an input's and — once
  `main` is in (§9) — for a SANS-days line. `if (page && ownPageRefused(page, 'line', o, again)) return` — the line
  unread, the list open, nothing said but the note (D2627 A11).
- **A holiday's line** (the `oilhol` branch) asks first as a line that opens a page does — `ownSquadronRefused('line',
  o, again)`, the same test with no page — and then hands its list to the door: `ownSquadronFirst({ door: 'line', ...o
  }, () => setOilList(…))`. On his own squadron nothing is asked and the list opens as today.
- An input's line ends in `openInputOnPage(n.iid, undefined, () => stillHis(n), { door: 'line', ...o })`. A line that
  opens neither a page nor a list goes on exactly as today.
- **His own SANS reminder** (§4.1 row 9b): its branch runs `enterForLine(homeOn(me(), today), again, 'line', o)`
  first — the line has no `n.u` — and returns on `'asked'` or `'refused'`; then `ownPageRefused('inputs', …)`; only
  then its four marks (read, the list, the SANS tab and month, `SANSDAYASK`) and the page. R5 (a) gains the caller.
- **`lineOpens` knows every kind by name, and an unknown kind never falls through as an input's** (Opus F-g, Fable 8:
  today `openNotice`'s last branch is `inpById(n.iid)` and its miss toasts "That input is gone" — which is what a tap
  on part V's Plan lines would say, built as written). Part V's two new kinds are named in it in the commit that adds
  them — the Plan lines (§19.2) and the callsign line (§19.4), each with the tap §19 gives it — and B7 and R5 gain
  them then. **R5 (d), new:** every notice kind a non-test file writes or works out is a key of `lineOpens`' own
  table, read from the source; a kind with no key fails with "say what a tap on this line opens".

**`openBellRequest`**: `'refused'` answers true (the tap was taken), `door: 'bell'`; then `ownPageRefused('admin',
'bell')` before `open()`. **`bellTap`'s OIL branch** sets nothing before the page — its toast and its question are
`first`, inside the door's `then` — so it asks nothing itself: `enterForLine(inputSqn(r, pageUnit('own')), again,
'bell', o)`, then `openInputOnPage(iid, first, still, { door: 'bell' })` (§5.3).

### 4.6 The note, and how `ui/toast.ts placeToast` learns the control

**The note never covers a control it names (chat 191's call, 26 Nov 26; found drawing the phone Calendar notes).** On a
phone the note stands at the foot (D2590). Where that place would cover a control its words name — a button they point
to (Save, Cancel, Add, Create…, Close, Retry, a window's ✕) or a page control they name — it stands just above the
highest such control: its foot 8px above that control's top, 12px from each side as at the foot, checked again at each
new place until it covers none. Controls it does not name (a calendar's days, a list's rows) and plain text it may
cover for its few seconds. Its test: the phone Calendar's "holiday with no leave period" note (drawing
`s1-cal-noleave-p`) clears the form's Cancel, Save and "Create the 2028 leave period". The ✕ check below stands as well.

**The words** — one table in `ui/guard.ts`, `guardWords(reason, door, to)`: **"Blocked: " + a lead + ", then " + an
ending.** The number is the squadron's (`unitById(to)?.num`). Every test reads the leads and endings from the table's
own constants, so a change of words moves no test.

| The lead — what stands in his way | Words | Status |
|---|---|---|
| a window open | "close the open window" | `[ruled]` D2590 |
| a window on a page out of sight | "close the open window on the Leave War" · "…on the Tracker" | `[ruled]` D2627 A7 |
| a Tracker window behind its Flow chart tab (N12) | "close the open window on the Tracker’s Info tab" | `[WNS]` — the reviser's draft, read by no one; built only on N12's answer |
| an opened row with a change | "save or cancel the open row" | `[ruled]` D2627 A6 (it replaces D2595's draft "close the open row": the row has Save and Cancel, no Close) |
| …on another Admin section, out of sight | "save or cancel the open row on Users" | `[ruled]` D2627 A6; "…on Squadrons", for a squadron's move or a place: `[WNS]` |
| the Give access form | "finish or cancel giving access" | `[ruled]` D2627 A6 |
| "+ New squadron" | "save or cancel the new squadron" | `[ruled]` D2627 A6 |
| Add a person | "add or cancel the new person" | `[WNS]` — `words.mjs addPersonNote`, as a fresh Fable left it on 25 Nov; the draft in D2598 and D2627 A6 read "add the person or cancel". **`[ruled]` since 26 Nov 26: the drawing he took as drawn (D2655 D1, the pair redrawn as N11) carries "Blocked: add or cancel the new person, then pick 149."** ~~Not his yet~~ |
| a settings window with a change (E1, default) | "save or cancel Inputs calendar settings" · "save or cancel SANS calendar settings" | `[WNS]` — the first kept by the second words review (r5b 1: the window's own title, its buttons Save and Cancel); the SANS twin is that review's gap 1b, read by no one. **Not settled:** on a phone the settings window lies under the Calendar, so the note names a window he cannot see — gap 1a, on §17's list to draw |
| the Calendar's unfinished work (E1, default) | "save or cancel the new holiday" · "save or cancel the changed holiday" · "save or cancel “Every Monday”" · "add or cancel the sheet's holidays" · "cancel the new holiday, or add its leave period" | `[WNS]` — the page's own drafts, none drawn, none read by a second Fable; the quote marks inside a grey note would be a first. With both a holiday half done and settings changed: the Calendar's note first, it being on top **(reading — words gap 1c)** |
| words in Quals' "Add qualification" box (§3.2 (i)) | "add or clear the new qualification" | `[WNS]` — this fold's draft, read by no one; built only if the reading stands |
| a change not saved | "press Retry to save your changes" | `[ruled]` D2627 A13 — "changes": the app cannot tell one from several; never the band's own words "Not saved" (D2594) |

| The ending — what he pressed | Words | Status |
|---|---|---|
| a pick | "pick 149." | `[ruled]` D2590 |
| to the member view · back | "switch to the member view." · "switch back to the admin view." | `[ruled]` D2590 |
| a bell line | "press this line again." | `[ruled]` D2590 |
| a tap on a lit bell | "press the bell again." | `[ruled]` D2627 A8 — true only because the refused tap leaves the bell lit and the request unseen (B9) |
| Undo of "+ New squadron" (E7, default — step 6b) | "press Undo again." | `[WNS]` — kept by the second words review (r5b 2): the bell's ruled sentence with the press named. **It joins only to leads that add no Undo step (that review's gap 2a — a real fault in the joining):** "save or cancel the open row, then press Undo again" is untrue if he saves — the save becomes the newest step, and "Undo again" undoes that, not "+ New squadron". So with this ending the row and form leads offer the one way that adds no step: "cancel the open row" · "cancel the new person" · "cancel giving access" · "cancel the new squadron" (drafts, `[WNS]`); "close the open window" joins as it is. **Not settled:** what a Retry that lands leaves on top of the Undo list — the builder reads it before the Retry lead is joined to this ending; Ctrl+Z gets the same words (gap 2b, said by no one yet); a window on a page out of sight, "…on the Leave War, then press Undo again." (gap 2c, not drawn) |
| a press that is none of these — a card of the OIL list; any later button that would bring his own squadron back | "try again." | `[WNS]` (words3 W4). A button with words on it names itself, as the page's A18 showed ("…then press Check my quals again."); the card has none. No press of his reaches this ending after Build 1 (§4.1 rows 10, 12, 13) |

**(reading), named for him — the page's E9n:** he was shown each lead with "pick 149" and each ending with at least
one lead; the note for a lead and an ending he was not shown together (an open row on Users, then a tap on the bell)
is the same two halves joined — **but for the Undo ending, which takes only the leads that add no step (the table
above).** No road reaches "back to the admin view" with a row open — the Admin page is not drawn in the member
view — so that pair is never said; a test asserts the state cannot be made (B18).

**One note at a time** (D2627 A10): `GUARD_ORDER` is the window, then the open row, then Retry — and every refusal
before the Tracker's question. Two rows at once (an opened row and Add a person half filled): the order of §3.2 (h)'s
table **(reading)**.

**Where it stands.** The approved drawings, measured from the script that drew them
(`scripts/handpass/n166/draw-beside.mjs`): `p1b` — the note's top 8px below the top bar's foot, its left edge on the
picker's (`#sqnMenuBtn`), the list closed; `p2b` — the same height, its right edge on the badge's (`#roleBadge`); `p4b` —
under the pressed line inside the bell's list where it fits, else 8px under the list on its left edge (the drawing shows
it under the list), the list still open; `p3-p` — at the foot on a phone, the ☰ menu closed.

**Where today's note stands, pressed (P3 1)** — the plain note every refusal would be without this build, "Blocked:
close the open window, then pick 149." raised by the app's own `toast`:

| Size | Window open | Today's place | What it lies over |
|---|---|---|---|
| phone, 402×874 | Input window | **the top** (D731 (1)) | **the window's ✕, 64% of it** — and at this size the window's title strip covers the picker and the bell, so no door can be pressed at all (also P2 3) |
| phone | Inputs settings, or Calendar | **the top** | **the squadron picker (all of it), ☰, Undo, Redo, Sync, the bell** — the very control the note names; they can still be pressed through it |
| phone | My leave (a Leave War sheet) | the foot | two rows of names |
| phone on its side, 874×402 | Input window | the foot — the desktop's 319px box; the app does not use its phone layout here | **the window's Delete, all of it** |
| tablet 768×1024, desktop 1536×864 | any | the foot | nothing |

**How `placeToast` learns the control.** It cannot ask "what was pressed last" (`ui/lastpress.ts`): the pressed row is
gone with its list by the time the note is raised. So the door says, and one table maps it:

- `ui/toast.ts`: `export type NoteAt = { under: () => HTMLElement | null; edge: 'left' | 'right'; below: 'bar' | 'self';
  inside?: () => HTMLElement | null }`; `toastAt(msg, at)` sets a module `AT` and calls `toast(msg, 'blocked')`. **`AT`
  is cleared only by a `toast()` that did not come through `toastAt`**, so the next ordinary note is back in its
  ordinary place. Inside an open batch `toastAt` is a plain `toast` (no door runs inside the publish batch).
- `placeToast()`: where `AT` is set and `noteAtFoot(from)` is false and `AT.under()` is on the page and drawn
  (`getClientRects().length`) — `top` = (the bar's foot, `under.closest('.topbar, .sb-top')`, for `below: 'bar'`; the
  control's foot for `'self'`) + 8; `left` or `right` from the control's edge; `bottom: auto`, `transform: none`,
  `data-at="ctl"`. Then, in the same call, the note is clamped 8px inside the screen's sides. Otherwise today's code
  runs untouched. The rise-in in `toast()` reads `data-at`: an anchored note drops into place, with no sideways shift.
- **The ✕ check runs at every place the note can stand** (P3's table shows today's top and foot each lying over a
  window's own control). D2590's "never the window's ✕" is the rule: a place whose box meets a window's ✕ (`.floatwin
  .win-x`, `.airpop-head .x`, `.modal-head .x`, the ✕ each Leave War sheet draws in its `.bidsheet-hd` — one constant;
  the last selector is the builder's to read off the sheets, §14) is passed over for the next — under the control, the
  foot, and only then the top. **The top is a last resort no known case reaches** (D2627 A15 puts the note at the foot
  over Inputs settings and the Calendar, where the foot covers none of their buttons); the browser test asserts it is
  never taken at the eight sizes. A14's answer takes the note off the foot on a phone on its side, where it lay over
  the Input window's Delete.
- `noteAtFoot(from)` — **the one predicate for "a phone" for this note** (D2627 A14): the note goes to the foot **only
  where the press was inside the ☰ menu** — `from === 'drawer'` — **or the top bar does not show the squadron picker**
  (an upright phone: `ui/floatwin.ts phoneLayout`, 620 wide or less — its bell and its lines are a phone's, D2590).
  Wherever the top bar shows the picker — a desktop, a tablet upright, a phone on its side — the note drops under the
  control pressed. The door hands `from` down (`'bar' | 'drawer' | 'board'`): both lists share the door `pick` and both
  switch controls theirs, and the ☰ menu has closed itself before the door runs, so the note could not otherwise know
  (Opus's low finding). **(reading):** the width at which "the bar shows the picker" is read from the stylesheet's own
  rule by the builder, not fixed here; where the page names have folded into ☰ on a desktop (`ui/navfold.ts`) and the
  pick is made inside it, the foot.
- **With the "Not saved" band up** the note stands under the band, never over the Retry it points to; on a phone the
  pick form takes two lines (D2627 A13).
- **A control that is not there when the note is raised** (the bell's list closed by Escape in the same instant; the
  badge at a width that hides it): `under()` answers null or is not drawn → the foot. **While it shows**, the place is
  worked out again only on a window `resize` and when a shell window opens (`FloatWindow.tsx` already calls
  `placeToast`) — a note is not moved about while it is read (`toast.ts`'s own rule); if the control has gone by then,
  the foot.
- `ui/guardnote.ts` (new): the table door → `NoteAt` — `pick` → `#sqnMenuBtn`, left, bar; `toMember` / `toAdmin` →
  `#roleBadge`, right, bar; `undo` (step 6b, on E7's default) → the top bar's Undo button, left, bar — the foot on a
  phone; `line` → the pressed line (`[data-testid="bell-line-<id>"]`), inside `.bell-list`; `bell` →
  the bell on screen (`pops.ts BELLAT`, else the visible `.bellbtn` — `#sbBell` on the board), right, bar; `card` and
  `page` → the foot. It is the `setGuardSay` function. With no page (a headless test) it is
  `HOOKS.toast(words, 'blocked')`.
- The note is told to a screen reader by `toast()` as every note is (`sayToReader`); the keys stay on the control
  pressed (the picker's button, the badge, the line).

stores through `test-support/msq-world`). **Every press is the whole sequence on the real controls** — the list's
button, then its row; the bell, then its line; the badge alone — never a call of the guarded function: a press on the
way to the door can itself close the window. "Window" below is an Input window on Inputs with a word
typed, opened by its own door, unless named. All fail first unless marked **pin**.

Rows of its own: the document viewer over an Input window — the viewer closes at the press, the Input window refuses (D2627 A1); a Leave War window left behind the Inputs tab — refused, the lead "close the open window on the Leave War" `[ruled]`; a Tracker window left behind a tab — "…on the Tracker" `[ruled]`; the same Leave War window once the squadron in view no longer offers its page — the pick goes through (D2627 A7); the Tracker's marking window and the Calendar, as E2 and E1 decide (A16 holds the Calendar's cases) |

| B30 | when the show-only windows close, and when they do not — **at every door** | ALL AVAIL open, unsaved chart edits, a pick: the question; **Stay → ALL AVAIL still open**; Discard → closed, then 149. A pick refused for a failed save: still open. A save waited for, then landed: closed, then the pick. The squadron already in view picked: still open (the press is no change). **The same four cases run through the view switch, a bell line to another squadron, the page door (B27's state: Hunter in the member view on 149, the Tracker held, a line of his own 142 input — Stay, and Discard with the saver failing meanwhile, each leave Insights open and the edit mode on), a holiday's line (`ownSquadronFirst`) and the OIL list's card** (Astra 5, Fable 10: the pick alone did not show the page door closing them early) |

**`src/ui/msq-guard-note.test.tsx`** (jsdom — which control, which words, never the geometry): the door → `NoteAt` table
row by row; `from: 'drawer'` → the foot; `toastAt` then a plain `toast` returns to the plain place, and `toastAt`'s own
`toast` does not; the reader's region carries the words; the note's look is the blocked face (`data-face="blocked"`,
the lead "Blocked:").

**`e2e/msq-guard.spec.ts`** (new; **needs a browser** — jsdom has no layout): at 1536×864, in a browser started at 125%,
at 2560, at 1366 (the bar on two lines), at 1100 (the pick inside ☰), 768×1024, 874×402 and 402×874 — the note's box
against `p1b`, `p2b`, `p4b`, `p3-p` (top, left or right edge, within 2px): **under the control at every size whose top
bar shows the picker — 768×1024 and 874×402 among them — and at the foot at 402×874 and for the pick inside ☰ at 1100
(D2627 A14)**; **it never meets a window's ✕ at any place it stands — under the control with the window dragged under
the badge, at the foot with a desktop window dragged to the bottom centre, at the foot on a phone with Inputs settings
and with the Calendar open (D2627 A15; P3's cases) — and the top is never taken**; it is the front layer at every point
of its box; with the "Not saved" band up the note stands under the band and never over Retry, the pick form on two
lines at 402 (D2627 A13); a refusal moves neither the picker nor the badge (lesson 27); the three doors reached by Tab
and Enter over each shaded window answer as by mouse, and over Insights the pick goes through and Insights is gone; on
a phone, My leave, a panel window that leaves the bar free; **the squadron number's two fades (§18.4)**; **E4 (N12) —
Down days opened on the Tracker at 1536 wide, the window narrowed to 1000: the panel's box has no size (measured, not
read from a `hidden` mark), the picker pressed, and what E4's answer says — on its default the pick goes through and,
widened again, Down days is closed**; on E7's default the Undo note under the Undo button, and at the foot at 402. Each test builds its own state and asserts it
is there before the press (the order's §8 point 4). Every scene has three squadrons in the picker (D2630).

---

## 5. Part F — a squadron's inputs are seen and changed only on that squadron (D2621), and the Input window records the input's own squadron

**Part F is three things, built apart.** **The list** (§5.1) — his answer to the page's A17, which replaced the three
answers the red team had drawn up for N2 + N8: with another squadron picked, the Inputs page no longer lists his own
inputs of a squadron whose page he can still open, so the roads from 149's list, its calendar and the row's "OIL?"
button to his 142 input are gone, not guarded. **The doors** (§5.3) bring the input's squadron and its page into view
*before* the window opens; they close security F1 and F2 by the order of what happens, and grey nothing. **The root**
(§5.2) makes the window itself know whose input it holds, so a door nobody has thought of cannot write across; it is
built last, and only once the table of §5.5 shows that no press opens a window that would be born greyed — because
the only words a held window has today would be untrue there (§15, findings 12, 13).

**The doors and the root never share an answer** (§16, finding 2): a door asks `inputSqn`, which has no clause for who
is named in the input; only the root asks `letOpenOn`.

### 5.1 The list — his D2621

**Today** (checked on this branch): `engine/inputs.ts inputsListed(unit, pid)` keeps every input that touches the
squadron (`inputTouches`) **or names him** (`holds(r, pid)` — the reshape plan's C2, the agent's design, not a ruling
of his); its two callers are `ui/InputsPage.tsx inputsPageRows` (the List, its filters and look-ahead) and
`ui/InputsCal.tsx calRows` (the month, a day opened). So on 149 Hunter's four 142 inputs stand in 149's list and
calendar, open live under a bar reading 149, and Save, Delete and the row's "OIL?" button (`reviseOil`) write 142's
record from there (pressed, P5 N2; P6 A2 — an input he files from 149's page shows on both lists).

**After — on a page whose squadron is not his own that day, and only there** (D2621: "with another squadron
picked"): an input that does not touch the page's squadron is listed there only where it names him **and his own
pages can never be the squadron it belongs to** — every squadron of `inputSquadrons(r)` fails `state/perms.ts
ownPageMayBe`. `inputsListed` gains a fourth argument, the reach test handed in by its two callers (the engine reads
no accounts); with none handed in it lists as today, so the engine's own tests do not move. **The callers hand it in
only where `pageUnit('own')` is not his home today** — on his home page the list is today's, every input naming him
(Fable 5 of the third read: without that clause Saber, home 142 with a move to 149 saved for 1 Dec, files leave for 5
Dec on 142's page and it is gone from the page he filed it on the moment it is saved — it belongs to 149 on its day
and he can open 149's page; the same for anyone holding Plan of the squadron he moves to).

**The just-saved row pinned at the List's head follows the same rule** (Opus F-e, HIGH; read on this branch): the
List puts `pinned` ids on top from `INPUTS.find(…)` (`ui/InputsPage.tsx` l.282, l.326, l.499 — never through
`inputsPageRows`), and pins are cleared by a tab, a filter, the range, "+ Input" and unmount — not by a squadron
change; the page stays mounted across a pick. So Hunter saves his own 142 duty on 142's List with a Person filter on
someone else — pinned — and picks 149 with nothing open: **149's List draws his 142 duty at its head, its "OIL?"
button with it**, live before the root and born greyed under untrue words after it. **Two lines close it:** the pins
are drawn only from the page's own list (`pins.filter(r => rows.includes(r))`, the reach test with them), and
`setPinned([])` with `clearInpReveal()` runs where the page's squadron changes (an effect on `pageUnit('own')`).
F17; F13 asserts on what is drawn.

- Hunter (142's admin, Plan of 149, View of 150), 149 picked: his 142 inputs are off 149's List, month, opened day,
  look-ahead and filter counts — he can open 142's page, so they are there. An input naming him **and a 149 man**
  touches 149 and stays on both, as today.
- **A man who has moved** — Ranger, 149's since his posting, no right in 142: his own pages can never be 142's, so his
  old 142 inputs stay on his page where he is, their "OIL?" button with them, and open live there (`letOpenOn`, §5.2).
- Saber, a master admin moved 142 → 149: he can open 142's page, so his old 142 inputs are on 142's page only —
  **in the admin view.** `ownPageMayBe` reads the role in force (`roleOf()`, read on this branch), so in the member
  view his pages can be no squadron but his own, and his old 142 inputs are listed on his 149 page and open live
  there, as a moved member's do. **(reading)** — Fable 9 of the third read: the list flips with the badge. Built so,
  because the list, the doors and the root then ask the one predicate and cannot disagree, and in the member view he
  is a member; the other answer (the account's right decides, whatever the view) needs a second predicate in all
  three. F15 runs in both views; put to him with §17's list.
- **(reading), for him — his words were "a squadron he can still pick".** A man with only a View of a squadron can
  pick it, but a View never reaches its Inputs page (D2381, D2252). Read by the letter, Ranger — moved to 149, then
  given View of 142 — would see his old 142 inputs nowhere: off 149's page because he "can pick" 142, and 142's page
  is not his to open. So the test built is "a squadron whose Inputs page he can open", the purpose he gave ("so he can
  answer them").
- What does not change: the Input window's own rule for who may edit (`InputWindow.tsx foreign`, `mayEditInput`); a
  leave across a move on both squadrons' lists (D2261 — the rulings roll-call names it to him as a clash with D2622;
  not this plan's); the bell still lights on 149 for his 142 OIL question, and its tap now brings 142 into view first
  (§5.3).

### 5.2 The root — what is recorded, and what each read becomes

`ui/InputWindow.tsx InputEditor` notes, when a window opens, **the squadron its page shows** (`openedOn.unit = pageSqn`,
l.226–229) and is held only when the page later shows another (`held`, l.232). So an input of 142 opened while 149 is in
view by someone with rights in both is noted as "opened on 149": live, no band, and Save, Delete and the OIL answer
write 142's record under a bar saying 149; a later pick of 142 then holds it with an untrue band. The commit gate does
not stop it — he has the right in both (the security read's opening paragraph).

**`state/inputsqn.ts` (new) — two functions and one table.**

- **`inputSqn(r, pageSqn)` — the input's own squadron**, for every door and for the window's note. A new input: the
  squadron its page shows (as today: it has no other). A saved input: the page's squadron where the input touches it
  (`engine/inputs.ts inputSquadrons(r)` includes it — an input naming men of both is listed, and opened, on either),
  else `inputUnitOf(r)`. **It has no clause about who is named in the input, and never gains one.**
- **`letOpenOn(r, pageSqn, pid): string | null` — the root's alone:** the squadron a window may stand live on although
  it is not the input's own; null for every other window. **One case, and no second:** *his pages may never be the
  input's squadron* (`!ownPageMayBe(inputSqn)`) *and the input names him* — the moved man's old input, live where he
  is so that he can answer it (D2621 `[ruled]`; held from its first draw he could never answer it, and a tap on his
  bell goes to that question before it opens the list — round 1, Fable 3a). **An input he filed for others and is not
  named in, in a squadron he has since lost his right in — N9 — needs no case: no bell line can open it.**
  `state/notices.ts rightHolds` drops an input's line that carries its squadrons (`fu`) once none of them is his home
  and no right of his in one began before it (read on this branch, l.870–883; the round-5 page tells him so, and its
  presser's k4 bears it out); a line stored without `fu` is data already stored (D56). F19 pins it. **N9 is closed:
  the root waits on §5.5 alone.**
- **`INPUT_DOORS`** — one row for each door of §5.5 that opens a saved input: `{ id, home }` — whether that door
  brings the input's own squadron into view before the window. Every row is settled now (§5.3); the table stays so
  that a door added later must say which it is (R5 (a)).

**Recorded in the window's note**, worked out **once, when the note is taken** (`setOpenedOn`) — never again on a
later draw: a note worked out afresh after his own move at midnight would read the new page and bring a held window
back live (round 1, Fable 3d): `unit` becomes `inputSqn(r, pageSqn)`, and beside it `let = letOpenOn(r, pageSqn,
me())`. `held` is then judged on every draw, from that note against the page as it stands.

| Read today | Becomes |
|---|---|
| `openedOn.unit = pageSqn` (two places) | `unit: inputSqn(r, pageSqn)`, `let: letOpenOn(r, pageSqn, me())` |
| `held = openedSqn !== pageSqn \|\| …not offered…` | `held = (openedSqn !== pageSqn && pageSqn !== kept.let) \|\| …not offered…`, `openedSqn` now the input's own — so an input of another squadron is held **from the moment it opens**, by whatever door, unless the note let it open there; and a window let open on 149 is held if the page then becomes a third squadron's, live if it becomes the input's own |
| `heldWindowBand(openedSqn)` → `store.ts heldWords` | unchanged call — **and this is why the root waits.** `heldWords` answers the pick's band, "This is 142's. You picked 149 — close this to go on.", whenever `enteredByPick()` and the squadron in view is not the window's — true if he picked anything earlier in the session, whatever opened this window — and otherwise "This change could not be saved.", over a window in which nothing was tried. Neither is true of a window he has just opened, and neither was approved for one. So no press may reach a born-greyed window: §5.5 |
| `swapOn` — a second copy of "which squadron" for the swap question | reads the note's `unit`: one field (the security read's tidiness note) |
| `pickUnit` — whose people the Person list offers | `isNew ? openedSqn : (inputUnitOf(r) ?? UNIT)` — unchanged in meaning; named so the builder does not fold it into the note (a two-squadron input's list is its first day's squadron's). What a **new** input's list offers on another squadron's page is part G's (D2628 C2 — §18.6) |
| `heldByMemberView(openedSqn)` | unchanged call |

**What Build 2 inherits:** the note's `unit` and `let` stay; what a held window looks like changes there.

### 5.3 The doors

**Every door that brings a squadron into view runs the same three lines** — `enterForLine(inputSqn(r,
pageUnit('own')), again, door, opts)`; on `'asked'` or `'refused'`, nothing; then `openInputOnPage(iid, first, still,
opts)`, whose window the page door runs (§4.5). `inputSqn`, never `inputUnitOf` — which names the first-named man's
squadron and would carry Hunter to 149 for an input that touches his own page (round 1, Fable 3b). **Where
`enterForLine` answers `'no'`** (his pages may never be that squadron's) **the window opens where he is, as today**,
and the root lets it (`letOpenOn`).

| Door | `home` | Its own |
|---|---|---|
| **A card of the OIL list** — `ui/OilDayList.tsx Body openInput`; today `setInpEdit(r); setPage('inputs')`, the window before its page (security F1) | **yes** — D2403 names the OIL list | door `'card'`, `opts = { skip: ['oilday'] }` — the same `opts` to `enterForLine` and to `openInputOnPage`, or the page door and the landing would count the list the card sits in. The card sets nothing before the page, so it asks nothing first. After Build 1 a pick is refused while the list is open, and a holiday's line brings its squadron first, so the list is another squadron's only by a change he did not make |
| **A bell line of an input** — `openNotice` | as today: the line's own squadron is entered first (`n.u`, D2325) | §4.5 |
| **The bell's own OIL tap** — `ui/topstate.ts bellTap`, the `oilHit` branch; today no entry is asked for (security F2; pressed, P6 A4) | **yes** — D2621 `[ruled]`: "a bell line about such an input brings its squadron into view first" | door `'bell'`; `again` is `() => bellTap(from)` |
| **A holiday's bell line** — `openNotice`, the `oilhol` branch | **yes** — D2627 A19 `[ruled]` | for a Plan holder or master admin the line's squadron is entered as today; for one who only views the squadron picked, `ownSquadronFirst` brings his own back first (§4.5), then the list — so the list never stands under another squadron's number |
| **A row of the Inputs list and its "OIL" / "OIL?" button; a card of the Inputs calendar's day; the calendar's own reveal** — `ui/InputsPage.tsx openInput`, `reviseOil`; `ui/InputsCal.tsx` l.950, l.511 | **no — and none is needed** | after §5.1 a row on the page — **the pinned just-saved row among them** — is an input that touches the page's squadron (`inputSqn` is the page's) or a moved man's old one (live where he is, D2621); on his home page it may also be his own input of a squadron he moves to, listed as today. F13 holds that as an invariant of **what the List and the month draw**, on every page but his home page: no row whose `inputSqn` is a squadron his pages could be, other than the page's own. The button raises a question, not a window, so the root could never have covered it: the list's rule does |

### 5.4 The reader's two leads, and the documents

- **The Leave War's clash note** (`leavewar/ui/Chrome.tsx goClash` → `leavewar/sync.ts openInputWindow`: `setPage`, then
  `setInpEdit`). It could be pressed under 149 only while a war window held 142's war across a pick — a state no press
  reaches after Build 1, though a change he did not make still does. Its window is now the page door's `then`
  (§4.5): refused, nothing opens (B26); the root holds the window if another road is ever found. Also a row of the
  walk's roll-call (F6, an argument and not a test, stays withdrawn; B4 holds that every Leave War window refuses a
  pick).
- **The Inputs calendar's just-saved row** (`ui/InputsCal.tsx renderPop`: `saved`, `revealed` — the row it puts at the
  head of the opened day's list, found by `savedId` in all of `INPUTS`). Whether it outlives a pick and shows a 142 card
  in 149's day cannot happen with the day's window open (a pick is refused); with the window closed and opened again on
  149 it can be asked. F7 is written to answer it: save an input, close the day, pick 149, open the same date. **If it
  fails, the fix is `savedId` cleared where the calendar's squadron changes — one line; if it passes, F7 stays as the
  pin and nothing is changed.** Not verified by reading alone (§14).
- **A document chosen late** — the one write that does not stop on `held`. `InputWindow.tsx DocField`'s file box calls
  `state/docs.ts docAdd(f)` for each file chosen and then hands the ids to the draft; neither asks whether the window
  is still live. A person who opens the file chooser, and whose window is held before he comes back with a file — his
  move at midnight, a right ended — stores the file and changes the held window's list. No squadron's record is written
  by it (both Astra and Fable read it so: the file is kept apart, and Save still stops on `held`), but a file is stored
  that nothing will ever name. **The change:** `DocField` takes `locked` (the window's `held`) and its `onChange`
  returns before `docAdd` when it is set; the file box is cleared as today. F11.

### 5.5 The doors into the Input window — the list the root waits for

Found by a search for `setInpEdit(` in the non-test files (twenty-two places; the four inside `InputWindow.tsx` and one
in `inputedit.tsx` re-seat the window already open and are not doors). **Before step 8 of §10 the builder completes the
last column for every row, from the code and then by a press; any row where the answer is "yes" and no ruling covers
it is a question for him before the root is built — never a greyed window by default.**

| Door | File | Opens | Can the page's squadron and the input's own differ? |
|---|---|---|---|
| A row of the Inputs list, and its "OIL" / "OIL?" button — **the pinned just-saved row too** | `ui/InputsPage.tsx` `openInput`, `reviseOil`; the pins (l.499) | a saved input | **Only a moved man's old input** (§5.1) — live where he is, D2621 — **once the pins are drawn from the page's own list and cleared at a squadron change (§5.1, F17); without those two lines: yes, by save-then-pick (Opus F-e)**. On his home page, his own input of a squadron he moves to: its `inputSqn` is that squadron — **the root must not grey it there: `letOpenOn` is not the answer (it has one case); the builder settles this row before step 8, and if it needs words it is a question for him**. Today: yes, for every input naming him (pressed, P5 N2) |
| A card of the calendar's day; the calendar's own reveal | `ui/InputsCal.tsx` l.950, l.511 | a saved input | the same (`calRows`, the same list) |
| A card of a SANS day | `ui/SansDay.tsx` `open` | a saved input | not traced — the SANS calendar reads the page's squadron (the code roll-call's "checked and found clean") |
| A bell line | `ui/topstate.ts openInputOnPage` | a saved input | **Yes, where `enterForLine` answered `'no'`:** the moved man's own input — D2621. An input he filed for a squadron he has since lost his right in, not naming him: **no line of it is left in his bell (N9, closed — §5.2; F19)** |
| The bell's own OIL tap | the same | a saved input | no, after its door (§5.3); **on `'no'`** the moved man's: live where he is (D2621) |
| A card of the OIL list | `ui/OilDayList.tsx` | a saved input | only after a change he did not make |
| The OIL question raised after a save on the schedule | `ui/inputedit.tsx` l.1864 (l.1626, `giveBackCuts`, only re-seats the window already open — not a door) | a saved input | not traced — it is the input just saved, on the squadron in view's schedule |
| A chip or puck on the week or the board | `ui/interactions.ts` l.1389 | a saved input | not traced — the week lists the squadron in view's people, so the input should touch it |
| "Edit input" in the document viewer | `ui/DocViewer.tsx` `edit` | a saved input | as the list the viewer was opened from |
| The Leave War's clash note | `leavewar/sync.ts openInputWindow` (l.4641) | a saved input | only with the war held after a change he did not make (§5.4) — never by a press of his after Build 1 |
| The OIL question of what was just filed from the Leave War | `leavewar/sync.ts askFiledOil` (l.883; for several inputs it opens the OIL list instead, l.880) | a saved input | not traced — filed a moment before, in the war's own squadron |
| New input: the Inputs page, the calendar, the week, Medical, a SANS day, an upchit from the viewer | `InputsPage.tsx openNew`, `InputsCal.tsx` l.502, `interactions.ts` l.1359, `MedicalView.tsx` l.221, `ui/sansadd.ts`, `DocViewer.tsx` l.139 | a new input | no — a new input's squadron is its page's. (Whose man it opens on is part G's: D2628 C2, C3 — §18.6, §18.7) |

The same by a bell line to 149. On 142 with no pick: pinned, as today (a pin) | yes — today it is drawn at 149's head |

that this branch has not, `git diff origin/main...origin/<branch> --stat` for each open branch. **This branch
(`6b4471aa3`) is 198 commits behind `main` (`1993bed86`), 101 files under `src` differing: bringing `main` in is the
builder's first act (§10 step 0), before any pin is written — every "today" of this plan read on a file in the first
row below is read again then.**

## 10. The builds, and the build order

**26 Nov 26 — he split the work into four section chats under a coordinator (D2656, D2657). §21 gives each section
its steps, its files, what it waits on, its tests, its size and its readers, and it wins over this section wherever
the two differ.** The two builds below are still the plan's parts: **Build 1 is the core section** (parts A, B, F, G);
**Build 1V is divided** between the Admin → Users section (§19.1–§19.4, §19.12) and the bell section (§19.5–§19.8,
§19.13); **the posting sheet (§22) is a fourth section**, the Leave War side only.

**The split recommended — the host decides and tells him (his D490: related features share one branch and one check,
sized by the riskiest; D485; D1087).** Two builds on this one branch, joined in the batch's one FULL check:

- **Build 1 — the guard (parts A, B, F), with part G riding (§18).** Each piece of G rides because the guard's own
  steps are not right without it, or because it is the same lines changed once: the show-only windows closing and the
  edit modes ending are the last step of the very doors that refuse; Add a person's Cancel is what the note's words
  name, and moving the form to the top is that form redrawn once, not twice; the list's rule (D2621) **is** his answer
  to the question part F waited on, and "+ Input" and "+ Commitment" offering only the page's own men must come with
  it, or an input he files for himself from 149's page vanishes from the page he filed it on; the "Welcome back" card
  at home removes a door instead of guarding it; the fades are told by the very entry the doors guard, and "none at a
  refused pick" is the guard's own rule.
- **Build 1V — separate squadrons, separate views (part V, §19):** Admin → Users across squadrons — another
  squadron's man showing only what identifies him and that squadron's access line (D2625), Plan given and ended by
  the squadron's own admins (D2626), the folded headings (D2629, D2631, D2635), the callsign note (D2632 as narrowed
  by D2633) — and the bell's number first on every line (D2624), bug reports (D2628 C5), the name badge (C7), the
  sign-off boxes (C8). **Why a build of its own:** none of it touches a door of the guard; D2626 changes who may give
  a right and C5 makes bug reports saved records for the first time — risks of a different kind from the guard's, each
  wanting its own readers (the security reader on D2625 and D2626: D1411), and a fault the FULL check finds should
  point at one build; it waits on drawings and words, not on N6–N13, so it can be built while those are answered; and
  about 46 hours added to about 71 would be one build of 117 under one set of reads (§13). **Order:** Build 1 through its
  step 1b first — both builds edit `ui/UsersPanel.tsx`, and 1b moves and reshapes the form at its foot — then 1V may
  run beside Build 1's later steps, in this chat or a parallel one that messages first (`shipping.md`).
- **One FULL check for the batch** with [MSQ-NIGHT-153-OPEN], after Builds 2 and 3 (D485), sized by the riskiest
  change in it — D2626 (permissions) and part F (saved records) — unless he wants Build 1 live on its own, which needs
  its own FULL check and the three conditions of §1.
- **D2634 — a person's postings on Admin → Users — rides the Transfer step (batch 4, [XFER]), not either of these.** **With it ride round 6's N6
  ("Post out…" on Admin → Users), N7 ("Ask 142 to post him to 149") and N9 ("It's him"), and the Admin → Users side of
  the posting sheet — the form inside the man's row (D2652, D2653 c).**

**Every step:** its tests written first and **seen failing** — except the tests marked **pin**, which are written first
and **seen passing on today's code**, and say so in their names (a test that will not fail, and is not a pin, is
reported, never bent); then the step; then, on his PC, **only the touched test files and `npm run build`** (the type
check — `tsc --noEmit` is hollow here), `npm run lint:changed`; the full suite is GitHub's run of the pull request
(`shipping.md`). A browser test file builds the app, so it waits for the checks lock. **Each commit leaves the app
working and the whole suite green**: an existing test whose meaning a door changes is changed in that door's own step,
never before it (§15, finding 9). **No step leaves a door half-guarded:** the page door, its callers' `then` and the
landing's second ask are one commit (step 5; §16, finding 1). **A step that draws something new starts only after
its drawing has been seen by him** (D1625), and is checked against that drawing as it is built (D1494).
