## 11. The risks, each with how the plan answers it

| # | Risk | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A window that opens after the guard has looked.** | With no question the check and the change are one synchronous call — nothing can open between. With the Tracker's question up, its shade stops the mouse and it holds Tab (`Modals.jsx trapTab`); what can still arrive is a save failing, or a window that opens by itself. So every door asks the guard **again** after the answer (§4.2 step 5; B19). The cost — chart edits discarded, then a refusal — is real and rare; said on the evidence sheet |
| 2 | **Two switches at once.** | A second door under the Tracker's question is already dropped (`core.js ask`: `if (dlg) return false`). Without a question each door is synchronous and `enterSquadron` refuses re-entry (`ENTERING`). A pick then the badge in one breath are two whole presses in order. The press report's "two picks back to back" stays as P1's twin |
| 3 | **A refusal that leaves a half-entered squadron, or a half-done press.** | The check is the first line after "is this a change at all" in each door, before any flag is set. The page door stands before `view.setPage`, which itself closes ALL AVAIL, Changes and Traffic — so nothing has changed when it refuses; each caller that sets something before the page asks first, and what a caller would open after the page the door runs itself (§4.5's table — each with a test: the line unread, the card unspent, no window, the Undo not done). B1 asserts the war's world and the Tracker's door as well as `UNIT`; B8 that nothing asked of the Leave War is left to fire later |
| 4 | **The note pointing at a control that has scrolled or folded away.** | The top bar is fixed, so the picker and the badge do not scroll. A control not on the page or not drawn when the note is raised → the foot. The place is worked out again only on a resize or a window opening; then, the control gone → the foot. The e2e resizes across the width at which the bar stops showing the picker, the note up |
| 5 | **A refused door that later runs by itself.** | A refusal stores nothing — no "wanted squadron", no callback. B10 runs the timers after Retry lands and asserts nothing moved. Today's two "runs later" roads (a held war window's Close letting the war in; the Tracker following once held work is gone) can no longer be started by a press, and stay for midnight (P5). H8's wait is the one place a press runs after a pause: it is the same press, only after its own save, it starts again from step 1, and it is dropped if he has pressed anything else (H8) |
| 6 | **A guard that refuses with nothing he can close — the trap.** | Six causes, each named by a reader and each closed in the design, not by a way out. (a) *A flag that outlives its drawing* (the four template dialogs, Changes) — every row reads the predicate its component draws from; R1, A10. (b) *A list that holds what does not count* (the question sheets' list and the document viewer) — the row is withdrawn; P10. (c) *The Tracker's state behind a card or a cover* — the bridge answers none unless the Tracker is drawn; A11. (d) *A window on a page out of sight* — it refuses and the note names the page; where the page is no longer offered it does not count (D2627 A7; §3.3; A7, B4). (e) *A reported row whose component unmounted* — `useGuardWork` leaves in its effect's clean-up, the only way a component goes; A6. (f) *A Tracker window behind its own Flow chart tab* — no trap, the Info tab is one press away, but a note naming nothing he can see: N12, nothing of it built until he answers; A13, e2e. **Logout and a reload still end any trap, but each loses what is typed — they are not the answer to one** (Astra). The FULL check's walk tries to make each by presses |
| 7 | **The Tracker's own windows.** | One read-only table in `core.js` answers the guard; `endSession` is untouched, so the sign-out's clean-up cannot be broken by this build (P17); R4 ties the table to it and to the components, occurrence by occurrence. The marking window closes at any press outside it — N7 decides whether it counts, and B4 presses the whole sequence either way |
| 8 | **A helper window another branch adds.** | R1–R4 fail on a new flag in `pops.ts` or `view.ts`, a new `FloatWin` id, a new dialog or window hook **in any file, counted file or not**, and a new window state in the Tracker — until it has a row; R7 proves the scans by planting one. The `?timing` readout is the worked case (§9). Two limits remain, said: a form with typing and no frame is seen by no scan (§3.1); and a branch built before Build 1 merges adds a window, and the test only fires once both are on `main` — the second to merge brings `main` in and meets it then |
| 9 | **37 test files change meaning.** | Step 0 is a census before any code, sorted into three named kinds, reported to him by name; each file changes only in the step of its own door. The checker's allow-list is the only place a raw entry is excused, and Build 2 empties it |
| 10 | **The checker makes an unrelated test fail.** | It is switched on only when every door is guarded (step 6), so it can only fire where a test drives a pick, a switch or a landing with something open — which is a finding about that test or a door this plan missed. Either is reported; neither is silenced outside the allow-list |
| 11 | **`switchRoleView`, `pickSquadron` and the page door become asynchronous where they ask.** | Only where they asked or waited; with nothing to ask all stay synchronous, so the tests and callers that read the result in the same tick (P1, P11, the 37 files) are unaffected. The page door answers at once — `'went'`, `'asked'` or `'refused'` — and where it must wait (the landing's question, H8's save) it holds the caller's `then` and runs it itself, so no caller has to read a late answer. H8's wait is behind `saveSettling()` and P14 pins the ordinary case |
| 12 | **The member view's hold comes back by the second entry** (§4.4 step 5). | B14 asserts the result after `whenUnitSettled()`. If it fails, the fix is in `trkMayHold` (the member-view half answering false while the entry in hand was his own press) — named here so the builder does not reach for a timer |
| 13 | **Over-refusal.** A window he would call harmless refusing makes the app feel stuck; a bell line that moves nothing refused for nothing. | His own answer took the six windows that only show things out of the refusal (D2596, D2597); the windows he was never shown singly are listed for him (§3.2's "(reading)" cells, §17). A line that changes neither the squadron nor the page is never asked (P16). The page door asks only for a page the squadron in view does not offer — never for a tab, never on his own squadron. The note always names what to do |
| 14 | **A press on the way to the door closes the window it would have counted.** The Tracker's marking window at the Schedules button; a bar-free window at any bar control. | Every door test presses the whole sequence on the real controls and asserts the window is still drawn after a refusal (§4.7). Which windows close this way on purpose is H2's and N7's; none is left half-way — counted, yet closed by the press that was refused |
| 15 | **The root greys a window he has just opened, under untrue words.** | The doors and the root are built apart (§5): the doors and the list close F1 and F2 (D2621); the root is built only when §5.5 shows no press opens a window born held, and each "yes" there with no ruling behind it is his question first — after the third read one row is left unsettled, his own input of a squadron he moves to, on his home page (§5.5's first row; N9 is closed) |
| 16 | **The note lies over a control.** | Each place it can stand is tried against every window's ✕ (D2590); under the control wherever the bar shows the picker, which takes it off the Input window's Delete on a phone on its side (D2627 A14); at the foot over Inputs settings and the Calendar, where it covers none of their buttons (A15); under the "Not saved" band, never over Retry (A13). The e2e measures each |
| 17 | **Build 1 merged with a road left open.** | §1's three conditions: row 21 named to him (it waits with group B, D2636); no open question built on its recommendation; every new look and word seen by him. F2 is closed on every road (D2621) |
| 18 | **Something opens behind a refusal** — round 2's first HIGH: the door says no and its caller opens the Input window all the same, live under the wrong number, beside the note. | The page door runs what follows the page (`then`), in one state only — the page asked for is the page shown; the landing's second ask answers false; callers that set a mark ask first (§4.5). Held by R5 (c)'s census of every call site, by the checker's second check under every test, and by B26–B28 |
| 19 | **A door and the window's note sharing one answer** — round 2's second HIGH: a clause meant to keep his own input live told the bell's door he was already where the input belongs. | Two functions: `inputSqn`, which every door asks and which has no clause for who is named; `letOpenOn`, the root's alone (§5.2). F0 pins the first; F1 stays green through step 8. He answered the one question with D2621, which leaves `letOpenOn` a single case |
| 20 | **The closed note's own "Open the Inputs page", refused by the sheet it is on** (Opus, round 2). Only in the held-war state — a war window held after a change he did not make, a squadron he only views picked: the page door counts the Leave War's open sheet, which is the closed note itself. | **Narrowed, not mended in Build 1.** The fix offered — close the sheet first — does not work: a sheet leaves `Sheet.tsx OPEN` in its effect's clean-up, after the redraw, so the door asked in the same press still counts it; leaving out one sheet needs the war's one row split per sheet, for a state Build 2's card removes. He is not trapped: the note is true (close the window), the sheet's ✕ closes it, and the landing then takes him to View-only Sched as today. A row of the walk |
| 21 | **A window closed for him that held something.** The rule "it only shows things" is his, window by window; a seventh window added to the closers on the agent's reading could drop a half-made change. | R8 pins the list of `'closes'` rows to the six his rulings name; the Calendar and the marking window join only on his answer (N13, N7); `closeShown` runs only when the change is going through (B30) and never at a change he did not make (A15) |
| 22 | **The list's rule hides an input from the one man who must answer it.** | The test is "a squadron whose Inputs page he can open", not "one he can pick" (§5.1's reading; F14's second half): a moved man given View of his old squadron still sees his old inputs. The bell still lights for an OIL question on any squadron and its tap takes him to it (F1, F14). F13's loop holds, for every seeded person on every squadron he can pick, that no input naming him is listed nowhere he can reach |
| 23 | **Build 1 live before Build 1V.** Between the two, his rule D2622 is met on the Inputs pages and not yet on Admin → Users: Hunter, on 149's page, can still Save, Suspend, Archive and Delete a 142 man's row (P6 C-users — against D2191 and D2625). | Named here and in the backlog item; it is not made worse by Build 1. If he wants the two live together, they share the one FULL check as planned (§10). **Since D2657 they are sections of one batch — merged into the batch branch one at a time and checked together (§21) — so the gap opens only if the core is merged live alone, and the Users section's first step (U1) is the one that closes it** |
| 24 | **The fade read as a fault, or missed.** A number that flashes at sign-in every morning stops meaning "it changed"; one that fades under a refused pick says the opposite of the note. | The tick is raised only where the squadron in view really changes after the app has opened (§18.4; G7, G8); "less motion" gets the steady white second, never a fade (G9). On a phone an open Input window covers the top bar, so the number cannot be seen while one is open — but a pick is refused then, so no change is hidden; a bell line taken with only a show-only window open closes it first |
| 25 | **A default built as if he had ruled it** — the third read's first lesson: the plan and the page he is answering had recommended opposite things on four points, and one test (F4) was already built on the plan's side. | Every switch point is marked **(default)** and names both answers and what each holds (§8's table); R8 lets a window close by itself only in a commit that cites his answer; a test that rests on a default says so in its name; the readings are §17's, each with its page letter |
| 26 | **A list of what is on the page that is not the page's list.** The pinned just-saved row was read from every input, so D2621's rule held in `inputsPageRows()` and not on screen. | The pins are drawn from the page's own list and cleared at a squadron change (§5.1); F13's invariant and F17 assert on what is drawn — a desktop's rows, a phone's day groups, the month |
| 27 | **Hiding is not refusing** (part V). A foreign row that only hides a field still sends it; a search that still matches a hidden sign-in gives it back. | The foreign row's Save sends the page squadron's line and nothing else, re-checked at Save (§19.1, V4b); the search is NQ3 |
| 28 | **D2626 opens Plan further than he meant** — a moved admin who is a member where he stands; an Undo or Redo by one who has lost the right; two ruled steps that pass it on. | `mayGivePlanOf` reads the role in force on the rows before the command, at every door, the gate and every replay, failing closed (§19.2; V5b, V7); **a replay by one who lost the right is refused whole, so no line says "taken back" while the Plan stands; the `first` mark is a master admin's "+ New squadron" alone; the re-link door asks the same rule (the security check of the fix, §20)**; the two-step road is NQ1, its three ways pinned by name until he answers |
| 29 | **Two section chats changing one file at once.** His D2656 has each chat "on files no other touches"; a handful of files are touched by more than one — `ui/UsersPanel.tsx`, `ui/postout.css`, `state/perms.ts`, `ui/AdminPage.tsx`, `ui/Drawer.tsx`, the guard's own table and the bell's line table. | §21's table of shared files gives each its order; the coordinator merges one section at a time into the batch branch and the later one brings the branch in first. **Three have no natural order and are named as findings there, not settled by a guess** (`ui/postout.css`, `state/perms.ts`, `ui/AdminPage.tsx`) |
| 30 | **A section built on a part of the core that has not landed.** A new bell line written before the page door exists is written "mark read, close the list, open" — the fault §4.5 removes; "Edit on 142 →" written before the pick's door is a pick with no guard; a new form written before the guard's table is counted by nothing. | Each step of §21 names the core step it waits on. A new bell kind joins `lineOpens`, B7 and R5 (d) in the commit that adds it; "Edit on 142 →" calls the pick's own door and joins R5 (a); a new form brings its row with it (R1–R4 fail until it has one) |
| 31 | **A new form the guard does not know.** Admin → Display has two sliders with Save and Reset; no ruling says whether a colour changed and not saved refuses a pick or the view switch. | A **(reading)**, on §8.5's list: it counts, as every form with a Save counts once something in it is changed (D2598, D2654 E1's settings rule) — its row and its lead built only on his word; without it the row is `'no'` and the scans still want it named. §3.1's limit stands: a form with no frame is seen by no scan |
| 32 | **A squadron colour that reads as one of the app's meanings.** An admin may pick the unread dot's blue, the warning red, the signed green or the posting amber for a squadron's number. | His ruling: no warning, the admin judges by the page's list and its preview (D2649). What the build still holds: the unread dot is always the app's blue (D2638); the brightness slider stops short of unreadable (D2652 N2 d); colour is used for a squadron's number on the bell and its Show button only — Help's reports, the sign-off boxes and Admin → Users keep the grey number |
| 33 | **A person's own setting saved for the first time.** The colours are saved with each admin's own sign-in — a new kind of saved record, as bug reports are. | Through the command layer; a gate rule that a person writes only his own; kept for the whole app, never in a store bound to the squadron in view; no Undo step **(reading)**; one more table on the database step's list (D473). The builder first reads where the bell's Show buttons keep an admin's own choice, the nearest thing the app has |
| 34 | **One search over five kinds of group.** D2647's search filters the block, Outgoing, Incoming, the squadron's own people and the folded squadrons; three of those hold other squadrons' men, whose sign-in names their rows hide. | NQ3 decides what the search matches for another squadron's man — now for every group he stands in; V35 asserts on what is drawn in each group, never on the model's list; a man who holds View or Plan shows once while searching (D2652 N13 d) |
| 35 | **A pinned row that leaks, or a control that moves under the finger.** The phone's search and jump rows stay under the top bar and Admin's left column stays put — nothing on Admin is pinned today, and the Users pane clips what is inside it. | "Nothing pinned leaks" (D1518) is checked at every zoom on both; a jump whose group a search hides goes grey where it stands and never moves (lesson 27); the phone's row at 375 px, with a group missing and with a two-figure count, has its rule and its drawing before the step (D2652) |
| 36 | **One man opened in two places.** A man with a posting out ahead stands in "People" and in Outgoing; a Plan holder in the block and under his squadron's heading. Two opened rows of one man could hold two different unsaved choices for one access line. | One opened row a page, as today: opening the second closes the first as its Cancel does — the builder reads how the page keeps its one opened row today and V32 pins it; the guard counts the one that is open |
| 37 | **Incoming and Outgoing drawn with moves the app cannot make yet.** The drawings show Cinch posted from 142 to 149; "Transfer to Sqn" is greyed until the Transfer step. | The two groups are built on the postings the app makes today — overseas, to SANS, to Delete, and a restore or an add with a post-in date ahead — and read a posting's target, so a move between squadrons shows the day the Transfer step can make one **(reading)**. The section's mock-up page says so in plain words |
| 38 | **The posting sheet's new look changes what the sheet does.** With a posting open the leave buttons are gone, so a way to file leave from that sheet goes; its head no longer names the day (D1462 narrowed); the main button changes from the grey chip he asked for on 18 Aug. | Each is his answer (D2653 d, f, b). The tests that press a leave button with a posting open are re-pointed by name, none deleted; the "Delete Cinch?" card (D1626–D1628) hangs from the same parts and is drawn in the new look before the build; ✕ and pressing the day again give the leave buttons back — PT3 |
| 39 | **A section shipped alone, then met by the batch.** The posting sheet may merge live on its own while the other three are still being built. | It touches the Leave War's sheet files and their stylesheet, which no other section edits — but for two rules in `ui/postout.css` (§21's finding 1) and the core's one-word marks in `BidPicker.tsx` (§21.5). The batch branch brings `main` in after it merges, and the coordinator runs the posting sheet's tests on the batch branch then — **and the guard's roll-call test (`msq-guard-rollcall.test.ts`: R3 counts the frames in the posting files, so a sheet or card added or removed by way C is met there, not in the posting sheet's own tests)** |
| 40 | **One FULL check over four chats' work.** A fault found at the end must point at one section. | Each section's own tests and its three code reads are done before its merge into the batch branch; the coordinator merges one at a time, each a named range of commits; the roll-call is kept by section (§21) |

---

## 12. The order's lessons (§7.11), against this build

| Lesson | Where it bites here | Covered by |
|---|---|---|
| 1 a reading in one state only | the note × door × reason × size; **a flag read with its window drawn, never with it hidden** — the first draft's own fault | §10's roll-call; B10–B12; A10, A11; e2e |
| 3 feedback, and what is out of sight | the note is the only sign of a refusal; the Retry it names is not drawn inside the ☰ menu (words-read N4); a window the note says to close that is out of sight (N1) | the menu closes on a refusal, from both its doors (H11; B2, B5); e2e asserts the band and the note in one picture |
| 4 the page behind an open window | the whole job — every non-shaded row | B4 |
| 5 a thing brought into sight against what stands over it | the note against a window's ✕, the "Not saved" band, the phone's keys | §4.6's ✕ check; e2e |
| 10, 12 a page hidden and shown; a window opened from every page | N1; a Tracker window behind the Flow chart tab at a narrow width (N12); the Input window from Inputs, the week, the board, a SANS day, the medical page, the document viewer | A7, A10, A13, e2e; §5.5's doors, walked |
| 11, 31 sizes; a real window at 125% | the note under the control | e2e at 1366, 2560 and started at 125% |
| 14 a list made by searching, never from memory | the windows, counted one by one; the doors into the Input window; every caller of the page change | R1–R5, R7; §5.5 |
| 19 a window over a window, closed by each way | "is anything still open" after each | A2, A4 |
| 23 the ruled tap beside the build's | a bell line moves him there (D2325); an input's own window from any day | B6's twin after Retry asserts the ruled landing; F1 on his D2621 — never the build’s own |
| 25 made where a person can put it | ALL AVAIL made by presses, as P1 made it (the demo offers none); every door pressed as its whole sequence | B4; the walk |
| 27 a control never moves under the finger | a refusal must not move the picker, the badge or the bell | e2e measures each before and after |
| 28, 30 a phone's press proved on screen; the first 100ms | the note at the foot, the menu closing | e2e in WebKit; the walk's pictures |
| 29 a page open across midnight | a held window, then his pick (N6) | P5; the walk |
| 32 a builder's reading passing as his rule | every place this plan reads a ruling further than its words | each is marked **(reading)** where it stands and listed once in §17, for the host to put to him; none is built as it stands, and a test that rests on one says so in its name |
| 2, 6–9, 13, 15–18, 20–22, 24, 26 | no gesture, print, pinned row or animation is built here | — said, not skipped |

**What only his iPhone can show** (lessons 6, 21): the note's place against the real keyboard, and against Safari's
folding toolbar when the foot is where it stands. Put on his look card.

## 13. Size — an estimate, not a measurement

**Build 1 — the guard, with part G riding.**

| Part | Hours | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Step 0 — `main` brought in (198 commits), the third squadron in the test world, the pins, the census of 37 + 13 test files, the last column of §5.5, **and the third read's reads and presses (the saver's end, the lull pop-ups, the list of windows for E9b, Quals' Done, Retry and the Undo list)** | 7.5 | was 6.5 before the third read: its reads and three more pins (1) |
| A — the table (a row for each movable window), the function, each drawn predicate moved into `ui/windrawn.ts`, the Tracker's read-only table and bridge with its two marks, the reports, the scans by occurrence, "counted means drawn", the closers and `closeShown`; **`dirty` rows, the Calendar's forms and its holiday sheet, the settings windows (E1); the Leave War's sheets told apart (E9b); the Tracker's closer widened (E2, E4); Quals' box** | 13.5 | was 9: E1 (2), E9b (1.5), E2 and E4 (0.5), `qualsnew` (0.5) — **4 of these 4.5 hours only on the defaults** |
| Add a person at the top with its Cancel (§18.3) | 3 | was 2.5: the second press's rule (0.5) |
| The note — `toastAt`, the anchors, the foot rule, the ✕ check, the words' two tables | 3.5 | was 2.5: the new leads, the Undo button's anchor, the Undo ending's own joining (1) |
| B — the pick (2.5), the view switch and the Tracker's question both ways (3), the bell and the page door with `ownSquadronFirst`: its three answers, `then`, the landing's second ask, the call sites by kind, six re-pointed files, the wait for a save at each door (7), the edit modes' end and the card at home (1.5); **`closeShown` moved into the final entry and B30 at every door (1), the SANS reminder's branch (1), `lineOpens`' kinds and R5 (d), Logic's waiting value flushed (0.5)** | 16.5 | was 14 |
| The checker switched on, with its two checks | 2 | the same |
| **Step 6b — the one Undo (E7)** | 2 | new: the read of the step, the hook, Redo's twin, the note, B24 — **only on E7's default** |
| **The saver's own give-up (N14)** | 2 | new — **only on N14's default**; without it the wait for a save cannot be built as ruled |
| F — the list's rule on the List, the month, the day, the look-ahead and the counts (2.5); `inputSqn`, the doors' table, the card, the bell's OIL tap, the late document (3); "+ Input" and "+ Commitment" on another squadron (§18.6, §18.7 — 3); **the pins, the home page left alone, the moved master admin in both views (2)** | 10.5 | was 8.5 |
| F's root | 2.5 | was 2: the home-page row of §5.5 (0.5) |
| The fades (§18.4) | 2 | the same |
| The browser tests and the records | 6 | was 5: the Calendar's cases, the Undo note, the pinned row at two sizes |
| **Build 1** | **about 71** | **before the third read's fold: about 54. The fold adds about 17** — about 9 for faults found (the pinned row, the SANS reminder, the closers' place, the list's home-page clause, the new reads and tests) and about 8 that are built only if he takes the page's recommendations (E1, E9b, E2, E4: 4; E7: 2; N14: 2) |

**Build 1V — separate squadrons, separate views (§19).**

| Part | Hours | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Another squadron's man on Admin → Users (D2625) | 5 | was 2.5: the row's own save, re-checked at Save (1); D5's short rows and the man with no sign-in (1.5) |
| Plan given and ended by the squadron's own admins (D2626) | 13.5 | was 6: the role in force at every door, the receiver's check, both directions of a replay, failing closed (1.5); the gate rules for the new bell lines, and the line the app writes at a move (2); the lines' taps and Hunter's home number (1); **the security check of the fix (3): a replay refused whole on the `stuck` hook, each line following a row left out, and the pass after a restore (1.5); the `first` rule and the re-link door with its words (1); the doors failing closed with nobody signed in, the tests that gave Plan that way re-pointed, and V5–V7's new cases (0.5)** |
| The folded headings (D2629, D2631, D2635) | 6 | was 4.5: the tick retired on E8's default, live people only, a fold over a changed row (1.5) |
| The callsign note (D2632 as narrowed by D2633) | 5.5 | was 4: "Someone new" as an answer, the greyed "Add person" and its reason, the line at the add, the request's twin (1.5) |
| The bell's number first on every line (D2624) | 1.5 | the same |
| Bug reports — saved, each with its squadron, who is rung (C5) | 7.5 | was 5: one store for every squadron, "seen" a record of its own, neither an Undo step, the gate's three gaps and the read rule (2.5) |
| The name badge (C7) · the sign-off boxes' number (C8) | 2.5 | was 2: the badge as plain words where he only views (0.5) |

**Both: about 120 hours, an estimate — about 35 more than the 85 the plan stood at before its third read (32 from that read's fold, 3 from the security check of the S1 fix).**
The three rounds of blind reads on this plan are done; after each build
one round on its code and its fixes — not sized here. **The round-5 page's questions, the three new ones and N14 stand before the rows they hold (§8), and
before any step that draws something new, its drawing and words seen by him:** the hours start when step 0 can.

**After the round-6 fold (26 Nov 26) — by section, still an estimate.** The four sections are §21's; a figure in the
first two tables stands unless a row here changes it.

| Section | Hours | What moved |
|---|---|---|
| **The core** — Build 1 (parts A, B, F, G) | **about 73** | was about 71. The fades on a phone, waiting for the ☰ menu and for the bell's list (D2642, N8): the owed fade, its two films, G9b (1.5); the button pair measured from the app (D2644) and the search row lifted to the page's top with the form under it (D2652 N13 i) (0.5). **Nothing is conditional on E1–E9 any more: the 8 hours "built only if he takes the page's recommendations" are built; N14's 2 are still only on its default** |

## 14. What is still unproved, and the lists

- **The plan's writers pressed nothing.** Every "today" is from the code, from the press report of 24 Nov, or from the
  six pressers of 25 Nov where cited. **Not pressed by anyone when this was written** (chat 176's presser is on the
  first seven): the Calendar window at a pick (N13); the marking window again (N7); a SANS day, the Leave War's day
  panel and Insights at a pick and at the view switch — what each does today; N9; N10 for a Plan holder on another
  squadron; N12 — a Tracker window opened wide, the screen then narrowed; Kestrel's view of a 142 man's row on 149's
  page (D2625); a window behind a tab the squadron in view then stops offering (D2627 A7's second half — read from
  `Sheet.tsx`'s kept-mounted guard and `perms.ts pageOffered`); a card of the OIL list pressed over a squadron he only
  views; Undo by Ctrl+Z, or from the Help page, on a squadron he only views; row 21 with a window open; the closed
  note's button in the held-war state (§11 risk 20); an armed "Discard N edits & load" at a pick; §5.4's second lead;
  §4.4 step 5's second entry; a press on the name badge on a squadron he only views (D2628 C7's own "tried before it
  is drawn"); a moved man's Inputs page (no screen moves a man yet — F14 moves him through the real door).
- **Read by the revisers, not run:** every "true in the code" of §15 and §16, and every "today" of §18 and §19. No
  test was written to prove a finding.
- **New design that no reader has read — this fold's, for the third read:** the `'closes'` rows, their closers and
  where `closeShown` runs; `ownSquadronFirst`; the list's reach test and its reading of "can still pick" (§5.1);
  `letOpenOn` cut to one case; the wait for a save ending at the saver's own answer; every piece of §18 and §19 —
  above all D2626's gate and the restore's rule (§19.2), and bug reports as saved records (§19.6). Round 2's own new
  design (the page door's three answers and `then`; the checker's leave and its second check; `inputSqn`; the `trkask`
  row) has still been read by no one either.
- **New design from the third read's fold, read by no one — there is no fourth round, so the code reads carry it:**
  `mayGivePlanOf` by the role in force, at the receiver's check and at every replay (**read once more by the security
  reader alone — done, its findings built into §19.2; what that fold itself added — `planRestoreStuck` on the `stuck`
  hook, the `first` rule, the re-link door, the lines that follow a row left out — is read by no one before the
  code**); the foreign row's own save; the gate rules for part V's bell lines and the line the app
  writes at a move; `dirty` rows and the Calendar's forms; the Leave War's sheets told apart; `closeShown` inside the
  final entry; the SANS reminder's own-squadron entry; the pins' two lines; step 6b; bug reports' home and "seen"
  records; N14.
- **Could not be settled by this fold, each named where it bites:** whether the saver has an end (N14, §4.2); the
  root on his home page for his own input of a squadron he moves to (§5.5's first row — a window that would be born
  greyed on the page he filed it on); what a Retry that lands leaves on top of the Undo list (§4.6); which command
  writes "Plan of 149 ended: he moved to 150" (§19.2 — this fold's answer is the app's own pass; the builder may find
  the line is better worked out by the bell, as the SANS reminder is); whether a 142 man can be placed on 149's
  schedule so that the OIL question after a save opens a window born greyed once the root is in, and
  `levelOfAccount`'s body (Fable's "not traced"); whether a signed sign-off box, and the published day, carry
  "· 142" (the page's D12).
- **Pressed by no one, added by the third read:** row 21 with a window open or a change not saved (the page's E7 says
  so); the two lull pop-ups; a new holiday no leave period covers, at a pick; the settings window under the Calendar
  on a phone; the SANS reminder tapped from another squadron; a just-saved input pinned, then a pick.
- **Owed to him, found on the way, not questions of this plan** (the questions page's foot box and the roll-calls):
  "Mark all read" on 149 also marks 142's lines read; a waiting OIL question takes the whole tap on the bell, so its

  (kept: its tap now brings 142 into view); the clashes with D2622 that earlier rulings make on purpose — a leave
  across a move on both lists (D2261), another squadron's sims shown on ours (D2181), a moved man's old inputs (kept
  by D2621 itself).

  `openInputWindow`'s road that is one redraw more than today between the page and the window, and in `snapView`,
  inside an Undo step, a `notify()` is held back until the step ends (`store.ts notify`, `deferEffect`), so the

## 18. Part G — what his answers add to Build 1 itself

Each piece: today's code (read on `6b4471aa3`; a file `main` has changed since is read again at step 0 — §9), what
changes, the tests written first (file `src/ui/msq-guard-g.test.tsx`, jsdom, `withThird()`), the twin paths, what must
stay as today. Where a piece draws something new, its drawing is seen by him before its step starts (D1625) and the
build is checked beside it (D1494).

### 18.1 A pick ends Logic's and Quals' edit mode (D2627 A4)

**Step 0 (D2729 Q2 — §21.1's block): the `qualsnew` reading below is his answer — words in "Add qualification" refuse the press and are kept; the other answer is not built.**

- **Today.** Logic: `state/auth.ts LGEDIT`, set by the page's Edit (`ui/LogicPage.tsx` l.442) and cleared by its Done,
  by `setSession`, and by `state/store.ts switchRoleView` (l.624) — never at a pick. Quals: the page's own state,
  `ui/QualsPage.tsx` `qEditing` and `qualsEdit` (l.304, l.316), cleared only by its Done (l.1157). Pressed (P1 5): after
  a pick 149's Logic and Quals stand in edit mode with "Done" drawn, though he never pressed Edit there; **Quals keeps
  "Done" in the member view** — a fault. Every change made in either mode is saved as it is made — **but for two the
  third read found (read on this branch):** Quals' "Add qualification" box (`newQual`, l.317 — saved only by Add or
  Enter: Opus F-c), and a value just typed in a Logic box, which is applied a tick later (`ui/LogicPage.tsx
  ruleApplyLater`, l.54; `flushRuleApply`, l.63, is what Done runs: Opus F-d).
- **After.** The two modes are rows of `GUARD_ROWS` that stay `'no'` and carry `end()` — **Logic's handed over by its
  page (`useGuardCloses('logicedit', …)`): `flushRuleApply()` first, so a value still waiting is written to the
  squadron it was typed for before the entry loads the next one's, then `setLgEdit(false)`** (without the flush, a
  bell line pressed with a Logic box still focused would write 142's 13h into 149's rules);
  Quals' handed over by `useGuardCloses('qualsedit', qEditing, …)`, clearing `qEditing`, `qualsEdit` and its armed
  delete — **never `newQual`: while that box holds words its own row refuses the press (§3.2 (i), a reading), so
  `end()` runs only with the box empty.** `closeShown()` runs each open mode's `end()` with the closers, so every door ends them in its last step
  (§4.2 step 6) — the pick, the view switch, a bell line or tap that changes squadron **(reading)**. A refusal ends
  nothing. A mode ended is not put back when he returns **(reading)**.
- **Tests first.** **G1** — Saber changes Crew rest from 12h to 13h in 142's Logic edit mode and picks 149: 142 keeps
  13h (as today), 149 reads 12h and **opens with Edit, not Done**; the same on Quals with a tick made; picked on to
  150, then back to 142: neither is in edit mode. A pick refused for an open window: the mode still on. **"13h" typed
  in a Logic box and, without leaving the box, a bell line to 149 pressed: 142 reads 13h, 149 12h** (Opus F-d).
  **"LOW LEVEL" typed in Quals' Add qualification, then a pick: refused under its own lead, the words still in the
  box, the mode still on; the box cleared, the pick again: through, and 149's Quals opens with Edit and an empty box**
  (Opus F-c; on the reading's other answer: through at once, the box empty). **G2** — Quals
  in edit mode, the badge pressed: no "Done" in the member view, and back in the admin view Quals shows Edit (fails
  today: P1 5); Logic at the same switch is a pin.
- **Twins:** Logic · Quals; a pick · the view switch · a bell line. **Stays:** every change saved as it is made;
  Done's own toast ("Quals saved"), not said when a door ends the mode; a change he did not make ends neither (A15's
  spy covers `end()` too).

### 18.2 The windows that close by themselves (D2596, D2597)

Their design is §3.1 and §3.2, the order §4.2; this is today's code for each, read so the third read can check it.

| Window | Its closer today | What a pick does today |
|---|---|---|
| ALL AVAIL | `ui/availopen.ts closeAvailWin` (`setAvailWin(null)`) | closes, in the one press (P1 2; `VIEW_RESET 'week'` at an entry) |
| Changes | `ui/changesopen.ts closeChanges` | the same (P1 6) |
| Insights | `setInsights(false)` (`ui/Modals.tsx` l.221–223) | shaded — no mouse reaches a door; by the keys: not pressed |
| A SANS day | none outside its component — `ui/SansCal.tsx` `dayIso` (l.150) | from the code, stays open and shows the picked squadron's day; being pressed |
| The Leave War's day panel | `leavewar/ui/daypanelopen.ts closeDayPanel` | from the code, stays; being pressed |
| A Tracker event's details | none exported — `core.js` `infoId` | goes at a pick and at the view switch (P1 4) |

**Tests first:** A3, A15, R8 (step 1); B4b, B30 (steps 3–5); P18, P19 (pins). **Stays:** each window's own ✕ and
Escape; what each does at a change he did not make.

### 18.3 "Add a person": one button beside Filter, the form in place, its Cancel (D2598, D2599, D2628 C11)

**Step 0 (D2729 Q10 and Q1 — §21.1's block wins over this section wherever they differ): while the form is open the opener is not on the page and the form is headed "ADD A PERSON"; there is no second press and no "on" look; the Home squadron box is gone and the form adds to the squadron in view.**

**His D2726 (26 Nov 26): the screen never shows two add buttons at once — the opener and the form's own "Add person". The form is redrawn through the design skill; K2a builds the redraw he has seen, not the lit opener above an open form.**

- **Today.** `ui/UsersPanel.tsx UsersPanel`: the form's state is the panel's own (`name`, `np`, `role`, `postIn`,
  `home`); it is drawn always open at the very foot, under every person and the Archived fold (`#accAddBlock`: the
  heading "Add a person", the Home squadron box where he is an admin of more than one, `PersonFields`, the sign-in,
  the post-in date, the role, one button `#accAdd` reading "Add person" or, once a sign-in is typed, "Add person and
  sign-in", and the note `#accAddNote`). `resetAddForm()` empties it after an add; there is no Cancel. Quals' "+ Add
  person" lands here by `p.openNew`: the block scrolled into view, the callsign box given the keys. At a pick the
  typing is carried to 149 with the Home squadron box changed to 149 (P2 1).
- **Round 6 (D2655 D1, D2644, D2652 N13 i) — this wins over the "After" below wherever they differ.** He took D1
  as drawn. **The place changed after it was drawn: the button stands in the page's TOP row — the search, Filter and
  "+ Add a person" on one row at the very top of Admin → Users, above "Waiting for access" (D2647's one search; D2652
  N13 i) — and the form opens under that row; the old "Add a person" block at the page's foot is gone.** On a phone:
  the search and Filter on one row, and "+ Add a person" full width under them (the Admin → Users section later puts
  its row of jumps between the two — §19.12; it adds a row, it does not move the button or the form). **Step 1b
  builds this place once; the Admin → Users section does not move it again (§21).** ~~On the People row after Filter,
  the form above the first person~~ — round 5's place, superseded. "Add person" and "Cancel" are one pair: the same
  size, on one line, measured from the app's own Save · Cancel pair on an opened row, never laid by eye (D2644, lesson
  103). The four small things of E9f are his (D2654). The note's words are his (D2655 D1 — §4.6). **Owed before the
  step (D1625): the form drawn opened at the top — D2652's own list names it; the drawing under the People row no
  longer shows where it is built.** In this step the search still filters what it filters today; making it filter
  every group is the Users section's (§19.12).
- **After.** A button "+ Add a person" `[ruled]` ~~on the People row after Filter (`.od-findrow`), full width under the
  search on a phone. A press opens the same form in place, under that row and above the first person~~ **in the
  page's top row, the form under that row — the round-6 bullet above**; nothing of its
  fields, their checks or their words changes. Its foot: "Add person" and "Cancel" `[ruled]`, side by side as the
  opened row's Save and Cancel are. **Cancel is `resetAddForm()` and the fold shut.** Quals' "+ Add person" lands with
  the form open and the callsign box ready — no scroll to a foot. The form reports to the guard
  (`useGuardWork('addperson', …)`) once anything in it differs from how it opens; folded, or open and empty, it
  counts for nothing — **and "folded" is only ever Cancel's fold or an empty form's** (Opus F-h: a fold that kept its
  typing would count for nothing, the pick would go through, and P2 1's carry — typing taken to 149, the Home
  squadron box switched to 149 — would be back). **So the rule is fixed here, whatever the drawing shows: a second
  press on "+ Add a person" closes the form if it is empty and does nothing if anything is typed or changed in it —
  Cancel is the one way to clear it.** **(readings, §17 item 8 — the page's E9f):** that rule; after a successful add
  the form clears and stays open, for the next person; the button keeps today's second wording once a sign-in is
  typed; Escape leaves it as Cancel does, as it leaves an opened row (D2396 (p)). While it is open the button wears
  the app's "on" look, so the form visibly belongs to it (the design review's drawing note). **The Home squadron box
  is NQ2's (§8.4):** on its recommendation the form adds to the squadron in view and the box goes; until he answers
  it stays as built and nothing here pins it.
- **Tests first.** **G3** — the button stands in the page's top row, after the search and Filter (~~after Filter on the People row~~); the form is not on the page until it is pressed; pressed,
  it is drawn under the page's top row, above "Waiting for access" (D2652 N13 i — ~~above the first person's row~~), with the callsign box focused (a desktop); **the search, Filter and the button are one row at the very top of the page, and no "Add a person" block stands at its foot.** **G4** — every field typed or
  changed (callsign, seat, sign-in, role, the post-in date — and the Home squadron, **as NQ2 decides: no assertion
  about what that box offers is written before his answer**),
  then Cancel: the form gone; **a second press on the button with a callsign typed: the form still open, the callsign
  there, `whatIsOpen()` the `addperson` row; with nothing typed: folded;** opened again: every field as a fresh form's; `whatIsOpen()` null. **G5** — Quals' "+ Add
  person": Admin → Users, the form open and in sight. **G6** — adding is byte for byte today's call
  (`addPersonAndAccount` with the same arguments, the same notes); the existing tests that type into the form are
  re-pointed to press the button first, each named in the report. The e2e: the button's box and the form's against the
  drawing, a desktop and a phone, "Add person" and "Cancel" in sight above the phone's keys.
- **Twins:** a desktop · a phone; opened by its button · by Quals' door; left by Add person · Cancel · Escape; one

### 18.4 The squadron number's two fades (D2620, D2628 C10)

**Step 0 (D2729 Q5 — §21.1's block wins): on a phone the bell's list never covers the number; the Input window a bell line opens does, and the fade plays when that window closes. Every "the bell's list over the bar" below, G9b's among them, is read so.**

- **Today.** The number is `ui/icons.tsx MarkInner`'s `<span className="k">{num}</span>`, inside `TalonMark`, drawn by
  `ui/topbits.tsx SqnMark` → `ui/SquadronMenu.tsx` with `sqnNum('schedule')`; nothing marks a change of it. Every
  change of the squadron in view goes through `state/store.ts enterSquadron` (l.989) — a pick, a bell line, the
  landing, his own move at midnight; sign-in comes through `resetSession`. `ui/scheduler/02-shell.css` carries the
  app's less-motion rules (l.134–138; a rule at l.309 dropping every animation — whether it is live is the builder's
  to read).
- **After.** A counter in `state/store.ts`, raised by `enterSquadron` only where the squadron in view after it is not
  the one before it and the entry is not the session's first (sign-in, the app opening on a remembered pick).
  `MarkInner` restarts one class on the number at each new count, in a layout effect — so it starts once the new page
  has drawn. The class: the number alone dims to about a third and comes back, twice, about one second in all, never
  fully out; no ring, glow, size or colour change; the TALON word and the claw untouched. With less motion switched
  on: no fade — the number bright white for about a second, then grey (a held class, not an animation, so the app's
  own less-motion rule cannot remove it). A second change inside the second restarts it, never queues. **None at a
  refused pick** — nothing entered, so nothing counted. A person with one squadron never sees it.
- **(readings, §17 item 7)** — which changes: every one after the app has opened, his press or not; which number: the
  top bar's only. **The refresh card as a trigger is Build 2's** (§17's second clash).
- **On a phone (D2642, D2652 N8 — narrowing D2620 and D2628 C10 as to when the fade runs).** The ☰ menu stays open
  after a pick (D2384) and lies over the number, so today's plan would play the fade where nobody sees it. **The fade
  is owed, not played, while the number is covered: `MarkInner` starts it once the ☰ menu has closed and the number
  is in sight — and the same wherever the number is covered when the squadron changes (N8 a): a bell line pressed on
  a phone whose list lies over the bar plays the two fades once the list has closed.** A desktop is unchanged. With
  less motion the white second waits the same way. One owed fade, never a queue: two changes behind the menu play
  one. Seen as a film and taken (D2655 D2, N8) for the menu; **the bell-list case is not drawn — filmed and seen
  before it is built (D1625).** Readings E9e's three points are his (D2654).
- **G9b** — at a phone's width, a pick from the ☰ menu: no class on the number while the menu is open, the class at
  its close, by a tap beside it and by its own close; a bell line with the list over the bar: the class at the
  list's close; two picks behind the open menu: one fade; less motion: the steady class at the close; a desktop: at
  once, as G8.
- **Tests first.** **G7** — the count rises once at a pick that goes through, at a bell line that enters another
  squadron, at the landing that brings his own back, at his own move at midnight; not at sign-in, not at the app's
  opening, not at a refused pick, not at a pick of the squadron in view, not at the view switch. **G8** — after the
  redraw the number carries the fade's class; a second pick at once: one class, restarted. **G9** — less motion (the
  media query stubbed): the steady class and no animation. The e2e: measured through the second, the number's
  opacity never under 0.3 and back at 1, its box unmoved and unresized (lesson 27), at a desktop's and his phone's
  size; the film for his look comes from this test.
- **Twins:** a pick · a bell line · the landing · midnight; motion on · less motion; a desktop · a phone. **Stays:**
  the mark at rest, the Schedules button, every size (D487).

His D2622, place by place, as he answered each (§17). Read on `6b4471aa3`; the tests are in
`src/ui/msq-views.test.tsx` and `src/state/msq-views-plan.test.ts` (both `withThird()`), written first and seen
failing unless marked a pin. **Every look and word here is drawn and seen by him before its step (D1625); the words
marked `[WNS]` are a fresh Fable's to read first (D2441); the points that waited on him — E6, E8 (V-Q1), E9g–l, D5, D8, D10 (V-Q4) — are answered (D2654, D2655), each
named where it bites; NQ1 and NQ3 (§8.4) and V-Q2, still undrawn, are open.** **Round 6 adds two parts — §19.12,
Admin → Users' page across squadrons (D2641, D2643, D2647, D2650–D2652), and §19.13, the bell's colours and Admin →
Display (D2638–D2640, D2649, D2652) — and part V is now built by two section chats: Admin → Users (§19.1–§19.4,
§19.12) and the bell (§19.5–§19.8, §19.13) — §21.** The security reader reads §19.1 and §19.2 with the third read, and their code after the build (D1411).

### 19.1 On another squadron's Admin → Users a man shows only what identifies him and that squadron's access line (D2625)

**Read with §21.2’s block “Step 0 — this part read against his answers” (chat 199, D2730), which wins wherever the two differ.**

- **Today.** `ui/UsersPanel.tsx`: `otherSqnOf(pid)` gives the grey number for a man whose home is not the page's;
  `rowLocks(pid, a)` — `rest = !home || adminOfUnit(home)` — decides what of his row the signed-in admin may change,
  **by the admin's right in the man's own squadron, wherever the row is drawn.** So for Kestrel (149's own admin) a
  142 man's sign-in, role, Suspend, Archive and Delete are greyed but shown (the 23 Nov hand pass); **for Hunter (142's
  admin, Plan of 149) and for Saber, on 149's page, Ranger's sign-in mail and Role are shown and Save, Suspend, Archive
  and Delete are live** (P6 C-users) — a 142 record changed under a bar reading 149, against D2191. `PersonSub` prints
  his sign-in name and role in the row's second line ("Ranger 142 · us · Pilot · CAT IP · Member"); `PersonEditor`
  draws the sign-in box, the Role box, `SquadronLines` (one line for every squadron) and the five buttons.
- **After.** One predicate, `foreignHere(pid)` — his home that day is not the page's squadron (`otherSqnOf` already
  reads it). **The row's face is D5's — his answer (D2655 D5, narrowing D2625 as to the short row and its press):** closed, one short row — his callsign, the grey "142", seat and CAT, and in words
  what he already holds here ("also views 149" `[WNS]`, beside the built "also plans 149"); no role badge and no dots
  (D2654 E9g) — **but his role in words after seat and CAT: "admin", or "master admin, full access"; a member adds
  nothing and a man with no sign-in has no role (D2652 N3 e, narrowing D2625, which had ruled no role on such a row:
  it comes back as a word, never as something to change) — "Hunter 142 — Pilot · CAT IP · admin · also plans 149".
  Inside his own squadron's folded group the row wears no grey "142", the heading already saying whose they are; the
  grey number shows wherever he is found outside his heading — in the "From other squadrons" block, in Incoming, and
  in a search (D2652 N4).** **A press opens it, as every row on the page opens: one line — the page's squadron's, "149
  None · View · Plan" — with Save and Cancel, and nothing else**: no sign-in box, no Role box, no line for any other
  squadron, no Suspend, Archive or Delete. **A man with no sign-in opens to words in place of the line — "No sign-in.
  Ask 142's admin to give him one first." `[WNS]` — and Cancel alone: none of today's other branch is drawn for him
  (`#accGive`, `#accGiveName`, `#accGiveRole` — "Give sign-in" is not greyed by the row's lock today: Fable 11).**
  `SquadronLines` takes the one squadron to draw. **Not taken — the other answers to D5:** the first way — the three buttons live
  on every closed row, a press taking effect at once, no Save (then the guard has no opened row to count, and a
  brushed "View" on a phone gives access unseen); and, his call by D2625's own list, a quiet "no sign-in" on the
  closed row.
  **"Edit on 142 →" (D2652 N5, with D2654 E8).** On the opened row of another squadron's man, at the right of Save ·
  Cancel, for whoever is that squadron's admin — Hunter and Saber for a 142 man, never Kestrel: one press picks 142
  and opens the man's whole row there. **It is a pick:** it calls the pick's own door (§4.3), is refused like any
  pick while a window is open or a change is not saved — this row's own line changed and not saved among them — and
  joins R5 (a)'s list in the commit that adds it; so it waits on the core's step 3. On a phone, where it does not fit
  beside Save and Cancel, it takes a line of its own under them (as N7 c has the ask do). For one who is not that
  squadron's admin the same place will hold "Ask 142 to post him to 149" — the Transfer step's (N7), never both.
  **Hiding is not what keeps the rest safe — the Save is (Astra 2 of the third read, HIGH; read on this branch).**
  Today the editor's Save is `accessWant(a, role, pickNow, lines, linesNow)` plus `renamed = name !== a.name`
  (`ui/UsersPanel.tsx` l.298, l.369), `name` and `role` captured when the row opened. So: Saber renames Ranger's
  sign-in on 142, picks 149, opens Ranger's row there, presses Undo — the rename is taken back under the open row —
  and changes Ranger's 149 line: Save finds the captured name differs from the account's and **sends the rename
  again with the line**, within Saber's rights, under a bar reading 149; the Undo's landing does not close an editor
  already on Users. **A foreign row therefore has its own save:** `saveAccess(a.id, { grants: { [page's squadron]:
  line } })` and nothing else — never `name`, `role`, `master` or another squadron's line, whatever the editor's
  state holds — and **at Save the row is judged again**: if his home is by then the page's squadron, or the page's
  squadron is no longer the one the row opened on, nothing is sent and the row closes with the words "This changed
  while it was open — open it again." `[WNS]`. The doors themselves (`state/accounts.ts updateAccount`, `saveAccess`,
  `archivePerson`, `deletePerson`) are not changed: on 142's page Hunter does all of it as today. Hunter's own
  "· also plans 149" note on 149's list stays — it is about 149 (D2400).
- **Tests first.** **V1** — Hunter on 149 finds Ranger by search: the short closed row; opened: the one line for
  149; Save, Cancel; **no `#accEdName`, no Role box, no `#accEdOnOff`, `#accEdArchive`, `#accEdDel`, no line for 142
  or 150** (fails today). **A 142 man with no sign-in, opened: the words, Cancel, and no `#accGive`, `#accGiveName`,
  `#accGiveRole`.** A 142 member with a sign-in: Plan greyed, "Plan is for admins only." **Blade, a 142 admin, on 149's page: his
  row ends "· admin"; Saber's "· master admin, full access"; a member's and a no-sign-in man's add nothing. Under "▾
  142" no row wears the grey number; in the block, in Incoming and in a search each does. **(These link clauses are V11b — the Users section's U3, after the core's K4 — not V1, and not U1's to build: the red team's Astra 4 and Opus 7.)** "Edit on 142 →" is drawn for
  Hunter and for Saber on Ranger's opened row and not for Kestrel; pressed: 142 picked, Ranger's whole row open
  there; pressed with the line changed and not saved, or with a window open: refused as a pick, nothing changed.**
  **V2** — the same row for
  Kestrel, and for Saber with 149 picked; and for Mako on 150's page: one line, for 150. **V3** — pins: on 142's page
  Ranger's row is whole for Hunter and for Saber, every button as today; Kestrel never reaches 142's page. **V4** —
  the line itself still saves: Kestrel gives Ranger View of 149 from that row — one Undo step, the words "Saved"; and
  the row with its line changed and not saved **reports its unsaved change (what U1 proves)**; that it then refuses

  Astra's sequence above, for Saber and for Hunter — after Save Ranger's sign-in name is the one the Undo left, his

- **Twins:** found by search · listed as a Plan holder · under a folded heading (§19.3); Kestrel · Hunter · Saber;

### 19.2 Plan of a squadron is given and ended by that squadron's own admins or a master admin (D2626) — a permission change

**Read with §21.2’s block “Step 0 — this part read against his answers” (chat 199, D2730), which wins wherever the two differ.**

- **Today — every place that keeps Plan to a master admin** (found by a search for the rule's name, PA1, and its
  words): `state/units.ts PLAN_MASTER_ONLY` ("Only a master admin gives Plan or makes a master admin.", l.158) and its
  two refusals — `setGrant` (l.350: Plan given, raised or lowered) and `removeGrant` (l.379); `state/accounts.ts
  saveAccess` (l.885: `if (plan && !actorMaster) return PLAN_MASTER_ONLY`); **the commit gate**, `state/perms.ts`
  (l.2818–2824: a grant row at level `admin` on either side, holding, needs a master admin — a person's delete and a
  verified replay apart); **the restore's rule**, `perms.ts restoreLeavesOut` point 4 (l.2388; `ctx.master`, handed in
  by the gate at l.2895 and by `state/undo-wire.ts` l.472, l.699: a Plan row is put back only by a master admin's
  restore); the row's own line, `ui/useraccess-model.ts lineState` (l.47–50: "Only a master admin gives Plan." — and a
  line that holds Plan locked whole for anyone else); the roles table, `ui/rolestable.ts` l.52 ("Only a master admin
  makes squadrons, gives Plan or makes a master admin."); and the tests that pin it — `state/units.test.ts`,
  `state/save-access.test.ts`, `state/piece-b-tables.test.ts`, `state/reshape-plan-audit.test.ts`,
  `ui/msq-users-row.test.tsx`, `leavewar/multi-sqn-fixread8`, `-9`, `-11`, `-12`. **An eighth place, found by the security check of the S1 fix
  (F4; read on this branch):** `state/accounts.ts relinkRightsProblem` (l.563) — linking a sign-in to another person
  drops every right it holds, so it ends Plan, and the door says "This sign-in holds a right in 149. Only a master
  admin can link it to another person." No bell line tells a squadron's
  admins: his own account's lines exist (`state/notices.ts acctWords`: "Given Plan of 149: full access to 149", "Plan
  of 149 ended"). `main` has since changed `perms.ts` and `units.ts` — read again at step 0.
- **After.** One new test in `state/perms.ts`, **`mayGivePlanOf(unit, actor, rows, today)`**.
  **Its definition — the security reader's S1 fix (HIGH; Fable 4 the same), read once more by that reader alone
  (the host's reading of D1411; `../briefs/2026-11-25-msq-s1-fix-security-check.md`: the definition closes S1; its
  seven findings and two notes are built in below — §20's last rows):**
  1. a master admin — the mark holding today on the rows judged, **and his role in force an admin's**: a master admin
     in the member view gives nothing, as `units-model.ts levelFrom` already reads the mark (K1, D2253); **or**
  2. **his home that day is `unit` *and* his level there from his home role alone is admin** — `levelFrom`'s **home
     branch, with `adminRoleHolds` unchanged**, and never its grant branch. It is the one body `state/accounts.ts
     adminNow` reads (K3, D2160: "the admin role, counting in the squadron he is at home in that day … an admin who
     moved squadron is a member there until made an admin again", l.415–424). **`adminRoleHolds`' own `first` path
     stays in** (`units-model.ts` l.335–339): a new squadron's named first admin, once moved in, is its own admin
     (D2191, D2247) and gives Plan of it. "Every grant left out", as this point once read, would to the letter strip
     that path and refuse him while `adminNow` calls him its admin — against D2626. What never counts is a Plan of
     `unit` held from outside it.
  3. **Never** the stored `role === 'admin'` beside a home: Viper, 142's admin moved to 149, joins as a member while
     his account still stores "admin" — judged by the stored role he would give and end Plan of 149 from the move
     day. **Never** his level by a grant (a Plan holder of it).
  4. **The actor is judged on the rows before the command.** At the gate these are the `before` rows
     (`unitRowsBefore(env.changes)`) and the actor's account before the command (`acct`) that `unitRowViolation`
     already builds for its own `adminOf` (l.2695–2709) — **never `w.rowsB` / `w.acctB`**: `worldsOf` answers null
     for a command that changes only grant rows (l.2137–2142 — every `setGrant`, every `removeGrant`, a Save of
     lines only), so built on it every give by a squadron's own admin would be refused at the gate, or fall back to
     the live rows unsaid. So one command cannot make its own actor an admin of `unit` and give Plan of it. At a
     door, on the signed-in account as it stands at Save.
  5. **It fails closed** — false for: an actor with no account, **at the doors too** (today `units.ts notMaster()`,
     l.172, is false with nobody signed in, so the door lets the give through and leaves it to the gate); a home it
     cannot read; rows not handed in; **an account switched off; a man archived, deleted or a placeholder** — the
     test `accounts.ts adminsOf` already makes of an admin who can sign in. `adminNow` reads neither `on` nor
     archived and the gate reads the session's role, so a suspended, archived or posted-out admin of 149 whose
     session has not yet lapsed would pass until the next repaint — and at the database step the server's gate is
     all there is. That window stands today for every admin act; it is closed here for Plan only. **(reading):**
     D2626's "that squadron's own admins" is taken as its admins who can sign in.
  A squadron with no admin of its own therefore has nobody but a master admin.
  **Who may hold it reads the same way** — the gate's own check on the receiver ("Plan on an account that is not an
  admin's", `perms.ts` l.2734) reads the raw stored `who.role` today; once every squadron's own admins can write Plan
  rows it reads the role in force too, or a forged give to a moved "admin" who is a member where he stands makes him
  an admin of `unit`. **The receiver is read on the rows and accounts after the command** — as `saveAccess` already
  does (`adminNow(next0)`, l.891), and as that check's `who` is today (`accountImagesNow()`): Saber's one Save "make
  Ranger an admin and give him Plan of 149" passes. The actor's "before" (point 4) never carries over to the
  receiver.
  **The `first` mark is a master admin's "+ New squadron" alone.** Today nothing in `perms.ts` reads it — it is read
  only in `units-model.ts` (`validGrant` keeps it on any Plan row, l.103; `adminRoleHolds`, l.339). Harmless while
  only a master admin writes Plan rows; once 149's own admins do, Kestrel's forged give of Plan of 149 to Hunter
  carrying `first: true`, and Viper's moving Hunter to 149, make Hunter 149's own admin from the day he arrives — an
  admin of 149 made at none of the places that make one (K3, K4). **So the gate refuses a grant row whose after-image
  carries `first` unless** the command is a master admin's `unit.add` and the row is that new squadron's, for the
  account it names — its verified replay too — **or** the row carried it before the command, same account, same
  squadron, still Plan.
  **Each of the eight places asks it in place of "is he a master admin":** the two doors and `saveAccess` for the
  squadron of the line; the gate for the squadron of the grant row, on either side; **`relinkRightsProblem` for each
  squadron the sign-in holds a Plan of that holds today** — its words then follow D2626: "This sign-in holds a right
  in 149. Only 149's own admins or a master admin can link it to another person." `[WNS]` **(reading — the sentence
  is this fold's, shaped on `planGreyed`; a fresh Fable reads it first, D2441)**. There is no hole there today (the
  gate's check on the dropped Plan row asks the same rule, and the door is the stricter); left alone it would
  disagree with the gate and with D2626.
  **A replay is asked too, in every direction (Astra 1 and Fable 3 of the third read, HIGH by two readers; security
  S4; read on this branch).** Today `restoreLeavesOut` returns early for anything but a put-back (`c.op === 'put'`,
  l.2396), its point 4 judges only a put-back whose level is Plan (l.2398), and the gate's Plan who-check excepts "a
  verified replay" on both sides (l.2821). So an Undo that **removes** a holding Plan row (the Undo of a give) and
  one that **puts back View over Plan** (the Undo of a raise) are asked only "is he an admin of 149" — which a Plan
  holder is. Kestrel, 149's own admin, gives Hunter Plan of 149; still signed in, he becomes 142's admin holding only
  Plan of 149; he presses Undo — Hunter's Plan is ended by a Plan holder. **So, in three parts (the second and third
  as the security check of this fix left them — F2, F1):**
  - **(a) Who.** `mayGivePlanOf` is asked of the restore's maker **as he stands today** for any grant row that is
    Plan **on either side** of the restore — before or after image, a put-back, a removal and a lowering alike. The
    replay's exception stays for the row's *day* alone, never for who: the gate's who-check loses "a verified replay
    excepted" (l.2821).
  - **(b) A step that fails it is refused whole — nothing written, no line struck.** Not left out row by row: a give
    is one command that writes the grant row, Hunter's own account line and the new lines to 149's admins. The
    timeline writes whatever the leave-out does not name (`undo/timeline.ts keptOf`, l.929) and blocks a step only
    when nothing is left (`LEFT_EMPTY`, l.956); `undo-wire.ts acctLineLeftOut` (l.481) follows his own account's
    line only; and the gate lets an admin's verified restore take away lines he wrote (`adminOwnerViolation`). So
    with the row left out the step would still commit: Mako, Saber and 149's admins read the line struck "taken
    back" while Hunter still holds Plan; the Undo of an end strikes "Plan of 149 ended" while Plan stays ended; one
    Save that lowered Plan of 149 and gave View of 150 is half undone; and Kestrel's list shows the step undone.
    **So the wiring gets one check, `planRestoreStuck` in `state/undo-wire.ts`, asked through the timeline's `stuck`
    hook** — the first answer a press gives (l.1173, l.1207), before `emptiedBy` — **over the step's own recorded
    images** (`entry.inverse` for an Undo, `entry.forward` for a Redo), never the list the hook is handed, which the
    leave-out has already thinned. It answers in today's words, with nothing new to write: "Something else changed
    this after your action — it can’t be undone now.", and "Press Undo again for: …" where an older step waits
    (`sayBlocked`). An Undo and a Redo alike. **Why `stuck` and not `restoreRefusal`, the hook the reader named
    (`masterRightRefusal`'s):** read on this branch, `restoreRefusal` is asked after `emptiedBy` (l.1180–1182,
    l.1213–1215) and refuses on every press, so every older step of his would stay out of reach behind it — the fault
    fix read 2 and D1263's code read took out of other steps. The reader's fix is taken — the whole step, through the
    timeline's hook, in today's Undo words; only the hook differs. The same check also stands in `restoreRefusal`,
    for the second ask inside the restore (l.1015), and the gate stands behind both ((a)). **(reading):** the step is
    blocked for good, as a step out of his reach is — a right lost by a move never returns (K3); one who is made a
    master admin later gives or ends that Plan by the row. **(reading):** D148's "says who" is not used — nobody
    changed the same thing; his own right ended.
  - **(c) What is kept, and the one put-back that is left out.** **Kept, each ruled — for the command itself and for
    a replayed *removal* only, never for a put-back:** his own squadron's admin making him a member, the Plan row
    dropping with it (`accountMadeMember`, D2250), and its Redo; a person's delete; an archive (D2311). **The Undo of
    a make-member puts a Plan row back, so it is asked like any other:** Ranger, 142's own admin with nothing in 149,
    makes Hunter a member and presses Undo — Hunter is an admin of 142 again and **holds no Plan of 149**. That is
    today's rule (`restoreLeavesOut` point 4: "that step's Undo gives back the role only" — chat 129's reading, told
    to him; the pin `piece-b-tables.test.ts` "4.") with `mayGivePlanOf` in the master admin's place: the Plan row
    comes back only where the one pressing may give Plan of that squadron — a master admin, or 149's own admin who
    made him a member holding Plan of 142. This is the one step (b) does not refuse: `planRestoreStuck` passes a Plan
    row put back by a restore that also turns that account from a member back to an admin, and the one body leaves
    the row out. **Each line then follows its row:** `acctLineLeftOut` already holds back his own account's line;
    it is widened to the new admin kinds by their squadron and subject, so "Plan of 149 ended: made a member" is not
    struck while Plan stays ended; and the pass that writes the opposite line after a restore
    (`leavewar/sync.ts acctNoticesReversed`, handed the step's forward images whole at `undo-wire.ts` l.666) is
    handed what the restore wrote — **not read to its end by this fold; V7 proves it either way.**
  **And it fails closed:** the restore's context carries `mayPlan` in place of `master`, and *not handed in* reads as
  *may not* — today `ctx.master === undefined` lets a Plan row back; a verified replay that puts back a row the one
  body leaves out is refused outright by the gate, as today (l.2896).
  `lineState`: for one who may give access to the squadron but not its Plan — a Plan holder of it, **a moved admin
  not yet re-made** — the Plan button is greyed, and a line that already holds Plan is locked for him, under "Only
  149's own admins or a master admin can give or end Plan of 149." `[WNS]` (`words.mjs planGreyed`, as a fresh Fable
  left it on 25 Nov). **The reason stands as words under the line, one at a time, the first that is true (the page's
  D6):** for a man with no sign-in, "No sign-in"; for a member, today's "Plan is for admins only."; for an admin, the
  new sentence. The door's own refusal says the same sentence **(reading)**, in place of `PLAN_MASTER_ONLY`; the
  roles table's reads "Only a master admin makes squadrons or master admins. Plan of a squadron is given by its own
  admins or a master admin." `[WNS]` (`rolesNote`).
  **The bell:** when Plan of a squadron is given or ended, one admin line of that squadron — "Hunter · Given Plan of
  149 · Kestrel", "Hunter · Plan of 149 ended · Kestrel" `[WNS]` (`planGivenLine`, `planEndedLine`) — written in the
  command that changes the right, so an Undo strikes it as it strikes any line (D1693) — **and only with its right:
  a step refused whole strikes nothing, and a row left out keeps its line (the replay rule's (b) and (c), above).** **Who is told — his answer (D2654 E6; it was this plan's reading 10): everyone who already gets that squadron's admin lines — its own admins, a Plan holder
  of it (Mako), a master admin with it switched on (Saber) — never the one who pressed; the man himself keeps his own
  account's line and gets no second. And Hunter's name wears his home's grey "142"** where his home is not the line's
  squadron, as his row does on 149's Admin → Users — on a bell showing several squadrons the line then carries two
  grey numbers with two meanings, "149" first and "142" after his name: drawn for him first — **and since D2638 neither is grey: each wears its own squadron's colour, "149" gold and
  "142" orange in the drawing (D2652 N1 a). The bell section's `BellList` draws both from the line's own squadron
  and its subject's home; this step writes the line and draws nothing (§21's contract).** **Not taken — the other
  answer to E6: 149's own admins only** — a new audience, which the gate must learn too (below); not a one-line
  switch.
  **The gate's rule for these lines — which the plan had not written (security S2; read on this branch):**
  `perms.ts adminOwnerViolation` admits a squadron's admin line to a reader who is not the actor's own squadron's man
  only for the war kinds (`oilhol`, `po`, `poback`, by `lineSquadron`). As it stands every one of the new lines is
  refused and its Save fails; loosened carelessly, any admin could put any words on another squadron's admins' bells.
  **So each new kind carries its squadron `u` and its subject, and is admitted only where the same command changes
  that very right:** a grant row of that account and `u`, Plan before or after, given or ended, and the line's reader
  one of `u`'s admin audience (`accounts.ts adminsOf` — on E6's other answer, its own admins alone); signed by the
  actor (`by`); one line a reader; never unsigned from a person. **"Plan of 149 ended: made a member"** is written by
  142's admin in the command that makes the man a member — admitted only where that command makes that account a
  member and drops its Plan row for `u`. **"Plan of 149 ended: he moved to 150"** has no command of the right: a move
  writes no grant, the reading rule ends the Plan at midnight. It is the app's own line — minted by the app's pass on
  the move's day, unsigned, admitted only as that pass (or, if the builder finds it equal in what a person sees,
  worked out by the bell as the SANS reminder is, and never stored); **no person's command may write it.** An
  archive's and a master admin's making: the same two shapes, their words not drafted. **His answer (D2654 E9l):** an ending the app makes is told, to the same people, the line saying why —
  "Plan of 149 ended: he moved to 150", "Plan of 149 ended: made a member" are drafts `[WNS]`, drawn before they are
  built.
  **What a tap on these lines opens — unstated until the third read (Opus F-g, Fable 8):** today `openNotice`'s last
  branch would read them as an input's and say "That input is gone". **Default, drawn for him with the lines:** the
  tap opens that squadron's Admin → Users at the man's row — under his own squadron's heading, opened, the row ringed
  (the landing a posting's line has, `useRingLanding`) — entering that squadron through the guard as any line does
  (§4.5: `lineOpens` answers `'admin'`). The other answer: the line is only read. Named in `lineOpens`, B7 and R5 (d)
  in the commit that adds the kinds.
- **Unchanged, each a pin:** Plan only on an admin's account ("Plan is for admins only."); his own squadron's admin

**Read with §21.2’s block “Step 0 — this part read against his answers” (chat 199, D2730), which wins wherever the two differ.**
